## Q1138. Finitely supported moments for positive pentadiagonal operators

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 12

Must every bounded positive pentadiagonal operator T on a Banach lattice whose order is induced by an unconditional basis admit a finitely supported moment pair?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 1138: Francisco Javier González Doña. PhD, Universidad Complutense de Madrid, 2023. Advisor: Eva Antonia Gallardo Gutiérrez. Origin for question 1138: Grivaux’s 2002 question, explicitly restated as unresolved in the dissertation. Setup for question 1138: A moment pair consists of nonzero x∈X and x\*∈X\* with x\*(T^n x)=∫\_R t^n dμ(t) for every n≥0 and a nonnegative Borel measure μ. Finitely supported means both vectors have finite coordinate support. Pentadiagonal means a_ij=0 when |i−j|>2.

**Source.** Francisco Javier González Doña. *Invariant subspaces for classes of operators: finite rank perturbations of normal operators and positive operators*. 2023. [primary source](https://www.icmat.es/Thesis/2023/Tesis_Francisco_Gonzalez.pdf) Location: Remark 7.5.5, p. 172; definition in §1.6.2

**Literature check.** Status for question 1138, checked 6 October 2026: Unresolved in the cited source; bounded literature checks through 6 October 2026 found no matching resolution.

## Q1236. Small-molecule characterization of every Δ-point

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2.10) · **Collection** 13

For arbitrary pointed metric M and μ∈S_F(M), is μ a Δ-point exactly when each slice S containing μ contains m_uv with 0&lt;d(u,v)<ε for every ε>0?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 1236: Triinu Veeorg. PhD, University of Tartu, 2025. Advisors: Rainis Haller; Vegard Lima. Origin for questions 1236, 1237: Conjecture 2.10 builds on Jung–Rueda Zoca; Question 2.1 restates Abrahamsen et al. (2023 preprint/2024 paper), §4.1’s final paragraph. Setup for questions 1236, 1237: Use real spaces. Lip₀(M) consists of Lipschitz functions vanishing at the basepoint, with Lipschitz norm; δx is evaluation. F(M)=closed span{δx:x∈M}⊂Lip₀(M)\* and m_uv=(δu−δv)/d(u,v). A slice of B_X is {y:x\*(y)>1−α}, ||x\*||=1, α>0. A unit x is Daugavet (respectively Δ) when sup_(y∈S)||x−y||=2 for every slice S (respectively every S containing x); “super” replaces slices by nonempty relatively weakly open subsets.

**Source.** Triinu Veeorg. *Daugavet and Delta-points in Banach spaces: Lipschitz-free spaces, their duals, and renormings*. 2025. [primary source](https://dspace.ut.ee/bitstreams/a5767c39-cc5a-4a49-9af9-61542cd9b95a/download) Location: Conjecture 2.10,p.37

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1236, 1237, checked 6 October 2026: 2026-10-06: finite-support/special-space cases known; no full resolution found.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2306.05536) · [2](https://ojs.utlib.ee/index.php/ACUTM/article/download/25451/19392/39375) · [3](https://math.ut.ee/en/news/photos-doctoral-defence-triinu-veeorg-daugavet-and-delta-points-banach-spaces-lipschitz-free)

## Q1238. Daugavet renorming without an unconditional basis

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.12) · **Collection** 13

Does every infinite-dimensional real Banach space admit an equivalent norm having a Daugavet point?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 1238: see question 1236. Origin for questions 1238, 1239: Joint-paper questions 4.12/4.17; thesis questions 4.1/4.2. Setup for questions 1238, 1239: For a real Banach space X, a unit x is Daugavet if sup_(y∈S)||x−y||=2 for every slice S={y∈B_X:x\*(y)>1−α}, ||x\*||=1, α>0; it is super Daugavet if the same holds for every nonempty relatively weakly open subset of B_X.

**Source.** Rainis Haller; Johann Langemets; Yoël Perreau; Triinu Veeorg. *Unconditional bases and Daugavet renormings*. 2024. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jfa.2024.110421) Location: Question 4.12; thesis Question 4.1,p.108

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1238, 1239, checked 6 October 2026: 2026-10-06: unconditional-basis cases and ordinary ℓ₁ Daugavet renorming are known; these targets remain unreported as solved.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 13

## Q1248. Simultaneous primal–dual 1-greedy Haar norms

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 7.1) · **Collection** 13

For 1&lt;p<∞, can Lp[0,1] be equivalently renormed so that the Lp-normalized Haar basis is 1-greedy and its biorthogonal Lp′-normalized Haar basis is 1-greedy in the dual norm, p′=p/(p−1)?

**Context.** Origin for question 1248: Paper questions 7.1 and 7.3; the latter restates Dilworth et al.’s earlier problem. Setup for question 1248: C-greedy means ||x−Gₘx||≤C inf{||x−Σ\_(j∈A)bⱼeⱼ||:|A|≤m}, where Gₘ retains m largest basis coefficients, for every x,m and greedy tie choice.

**Source.** Fernando Albiac; José L. Ansorena; Miguel Berasategui; Pablo M. Berná. *Isometric renormings for greedy bases in Banach spaces, with applications to the Haar System in Lp[0,1], 1&lt;p<∞*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.21733) Location: Question 7.1

## Q1250. A quasi-greedy basic sequence in every Banach space

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 9) · **Collection** 13

Must every infinite-dimensional Banach space contain a quasi-greedy basic sequence?

**Context.** Origin for question 1250: Source problems 9,15, restating the established research questions. Setup for question 1250: A basis means a semi-normalized complete minimal system (eₙ) with supₙ||eₙ||||eₙ\*||<∞. It is quasi-greedy when sums retaining m largest coefficients converge in norm to each vector. A basic sequence is such a basis of its closed span.

**Source.** Fernando Albiac; José L. Ansorena; Vladimir Temlyakov. *Twenty-five years of greedy bases*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jat.2024.106141) Location: Problem 9,§6

## Q1350. Circular symmetry of the localizable spectrum

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 14

For every ergodic weighted translation W_(φ,τ) on L^p(Ω,μ), 1≤p≤∞, must σ\_loc(W_(φ,τ)) be invariant under multiplication by every complex number of modulus 1?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 1350: Miguel Monsalve López. PhD, Universidad Complutense de Madrid, 2020. Advisor: Eva Antonia Gallardo Gutiérrez. Origin for question 1350: Explicit dissertation question. Setup for question 1350: (Ω,μ) is nonatomic probability; τ is an ergodic measure-preserving isomorphism; φ∈L∞(Ω); W_(φ,τ)f=φ(f∘τ). Set X_T(U)={f:σ\_T(f)⊂U} and σ\_loc(T)={λ:X_T(U)≠{0} for every open U∋λ}. Here σ\_T(f) is the complement of points having a neighborhood with an analytic solution u(z) of (T−zI)u(z)=f.

**Source.** Miguel Monsalve López. *Bishop operators: invariant subspaces and spectral theory*. 2020. [primary source](https://www.icmat.es/Thesis/2020/Tesis_Miguel_Monsalve.pdf) Location: §4.2, Problem, p. 105; σ\_loc definition p. 95

## Q1636. A nonclassical minimal space with symmetric basis

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 17

Is there an infinite-dimensional minimal Banach space with a symmetric basis that is not isomorphic to a subspace of c₀ or of any ℓ\_p, 1≤p<∞?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 1636: Svetozar Zlatkov Stankov. PhD, Institute of Mathematics and Informatics, Bulgarian Academy of Sciences, 2025. Advisor: Denka Kutzarova. Origin for question 1636: Folklore question explicitly restated in the thesis, pp. 7–8; not the thesis’s solved unique-subsymmetric-basis question. A Schauder basis is symmetric if it is equivalent to every permutation of itself.

## Q1638. Periodic shadowing across ℓp and c₀

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.13) · **Collection** 17

For the same real or complex weights w and every 1≤p<∞, does B_w have periodic shadowing on ℓ\_p(Z) if and only if it has periodic shadowing on c₀(Z)?

**Context.** Origin for question 1638: Question 4.13 in the September 2025 revision. Setup for question 1638: B_w(x)\_n=w_{n+1}x_{n+1}, with 0&lt;inf_n|w_n|≤sup_n|w_n|<∞. Periodic shadowing means: for every ε>0 some δ>0 makes every periodic sequence (x_j)\_{j∈Z} with ‖B_wx_j−x_{j+1}‖<δ ε-close, at every j, to the orbit of a periodic point.

**Source.** Michal Hevessy; Tomáš Raunig. *Dynamical properties of weighted shifts on sequence spaces*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.11852v2) Location: Question 4.13, p. 17; Definition 1.2

<a id="q1643"></a>

## Q1643. Uniform Steinhaus renorming of c₀

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 17

Does c₀ admit an equivalent norm with uniform property (S)?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1643, 1644, 1645: Authors’ §5(1), §5(5), and §5(4), respectively, in the June 2026 revision. Setup for questions 1643, 1644, 1645: Set U_X(d;a)=inf_{x,y∈S_X,‖x−y‖≥d} sup_{‖z‖≤a}|‖x+z‖−‖y+z‖|. Uniform (S) means U_X(d;a)>0 for all 0&lt;d<2 and a>0. For pointed M, F(M)=closure(span{δ\_x:x∈M})⊂Lip₀(M)\*, where Lip₀(M) consists of Lipschitz functions vanishing at the basepoint, with Lipschitz norm.

**Source.** William B. Johnson; Tomasz Kania. *Uniform Property (S)*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.09106v2) Location: §5(1), p. 20

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1643, 1644, 1645, checked 6 October 2026: retained in v2; Kania’s current research page explicitly still asks the c₀ case.

## Q1646. Exact big-slice renorming without the RNP

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 17

Does every Banach space failing RNP admit an equivalent norm for which every slice of its closed unit ball has radius 1?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1646, 1647, 1648: Questions 1–3; radius-one renorming and strongly regular existence targets extend earlier big-slice work. Setup for questions 1646, 1647, 1648: Spaces are real. A slice of C is {x∈C:f(x)>sup f(C)−α}, f∈X\*, α>0. Radius is inf_{x∈X}sup_{c∈C}‖c−x‖. RNP means every bounded set has slices of arbitrarily small diameter; CPCP replaces slices by nonempty relatively weakly open subsets of bounded convex sets; strong regularity replaces them by convex combinations of slices of bounded closed convex sets.

**Source.** Ginés López-Pérez; Abraham Rueda Zoca. *Strongly regular Banach spaces with big weakly open subsets in the unit ball*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12471) Location: Question 1, p. 4

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1646, 1647, 1648, checked 6 October 2026: July preprint proves approximate-radius results, explicitly retaining the exact-radius questions.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00009-026-03143-x)

## Q1735. Open convolution on an infinite group

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3) · **Collection** 18

Does an infinite discrete group G exist for which convolution ℓ¹(G)×ℓ¹(G)→ℓ¹(G) is open?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1734, 1735: Tingley (1987); convolution: Kania–Maślany, published paper III, §5 Question 3. Setup for questions 1734, 1735: For Tingley’s problem use real Banach spaces and unit spheres S_X={x:||x||=1}. For discrete G, ℓ¹(G) has complex coefficients, sum norm, and convolution (a\*b)(g)=Σ\_h a(h)b(h⁻¹g). Open means mapping every norm-open subset of ℓ¹(G)×ℓ¹(G) to an open subset.

**Source.** Natalia Maślany. *Combinatorial Banach spaces and related topics: algebraic structure and self-isometries*. 2025. Advisor(s): Tomasz Kania. ## Q1736. Frequent-recurrence category dichotomy

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 1) · **Collection** 18

Must FRec(T) be either all of X or meagre in X?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 1736, 1737, 1738: Antoni López-Martínez. PhD, Universitat Politècnica de València, 2023. Advisors: Alfred Peris Manguillot; Sophie Grivaux. Origin for questions 1736, 1737, 1738: Earlier: Bonilla–Grosse-Erdmann–López-Martínez–Peris (2022); Grivaux–López-Martínez (2023). Setup for questions 1736, 1737, 1738: X is a separable infinite-dimensional F-space (complete metrizable topological vector space); T:X→X is continuous linear. FRec(T) consists of x with liminf_N |{0≤n&lt;N:Tⁿx∈U}|/N>0 for every neighborhood U of x. Uniform recurrence replaces positive lower density by bounded return gaps.

**Source.** Antoni López-Martínez. *Combinatorial Number Theory, Recurrence of Operators and Linear Dynamics*. 2023. [primary source](https://riunet.upv.es/server/api/core/bitstreams/f2691b55-3a05-4aff-84c6-9c6a418bd95a/content) Location: Conclusions, Open Problem 1(a), p. 166

## Q1740. Exact complementation constant for ideal-null spaces

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5.12) · **Collection** 18

Whenever c₀,ℐ is complemented in ℓ∞, must λ(ℐ)=2?

**Context.** Origin for question 1740: Authors’ Question 5.12; λ(ℐ)≥2 is proved immediately beforehand. Setup for question 1740: An ideal Fin⊆ℐ⊊P(N) is closed under subsets and finite unions. Put c₀,ℐ={x∈ℓ∞: {n:|x_n|≥ε}∈ℐ for every ε>0}, with supremum norm, and λ(ℐ)=inf{||P||: P:ℓ∞→c₀,ℐ is a bounded linear projection}.

## Q1741. Asymptotic radius of the cube

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 18

Determine the asymptotic order of R_BM(ℓ∞ⁿ) as n→∞.

**Context.** Origin for questions 1741, 1742: Finite radius: Grünbaum (1960). Infinite-dimensional question: Schäffer (1976), with separable case settled by Johnson–Odell. Setup for questions 1741, 1742: For isomorphic real Banach spaces, d_BM(X,Y)=inf_T ||T||||T⁻¹|| over onto linear isomorphisms. Set R_BM(X)=sup_{Y≅X}d_BM(X,Y).

**Source.** Omer Friedland; Assaf Naor; Pierre Youssef. *On the Banach–Mazur radius of ℓ∞ⁿ*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21112v2) Location: Introduction, pp. 1–2

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1741, 1742, checked 6 October 2026: August revision retains both questions; known finite bounds are n^(5/8)/(log n)^O(1)≲R_BM(ℓ∞ⁿ)≲n^(2/3).

## Q1743. Locally flat separation beyond compact spaces

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 2.7) · **Collection** 18

For every purely 1-unrectifiable metric M, x,y∈M and ε>0, is there a locally flat 1-Lipschitz f:M→R with f(y)−f(x)>d(x,y)−ε?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1743, 1744: Separation extends Weaver’s compact problem; Euclidean-dimensional isomorphism is a longstanding problem restated here. Setup for questions 1743, 1744: Purely 1-unrectifiable means H¹(φ(A))=0 for every A⊆R and Lipschitz φ:A→M. A Lipschitz f is locally flat if lim_{r↓0}Lip(f|B(x,r))=0 at every x. For pointed M, F(M)=closure span{δ\_x:x∈M}⊆Lip₀(M)\*, where δ\_x(f)=f(x) and Lip₀(M) consists of Lipschitz functions vanishing at the basepoint, with Lipschitz norm.

**Source.** Ramón J. Aliaga. *Lipschitz-free spaces and purely 1-unrectifiable metric spaces*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02918) Location: Question 2.7; Definitions 2.1 and 2.4

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1743, 1744, checked 6 October 2026: June paper explicitly leaves both open; later non-porous-set results do not compare different ambient dimensions.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.02918v1) · [2](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jfa.2026.111439) · [3](https://doi.org/10.4995/Thesis/10251/159256)

<a id="q1747"></a>

## Q1747. Local lifting for a product of free groups

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 16) · **Collection** 18

Does C\*(F₂×F₂) have LLP?

**Context.** Origin for question 1747: Kirchberg’s older lifting problem; restated as Problem 16 by the 2025 workshop participants. Setup for question 1747: F₂ is the free group on two generators and A=C\*(F₂×F₂) is the full group C\*-algebra. LLP (local lifting property) means: for every unital C\*-algebra B, closed ideal J⊂B, unital completely positive φ:A→B/J, and finite-dimensional operator subsystem E⊆A, there exists unital completely positive ψ:E→B with qψ=φ|E, where q:B→B/J is the quotient.

**Source.** Stuart White (collector). *C\*-Algebras: Open Problem Session*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.4171/OWR/2025/35) Location: Open Problem Session, Problem 16, p. 1944

**Literature check.** Status for question 1747, checked 6 October 2026: Fournier-Facio–Willett (April 2026), Question 1.6 and Remark 3.22, explicitly retain this question; their chordal-graph result does not cover F₂×F₂.

**Further links.** [1](https://publications.mfo.de/bitstream/handle/mfo/4376/OWR_2025_35.pdf?isAllowed=y&sequence=1) · [2](https://ems.press/content/serial-article-files/47873) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.18456v2)

## Q1837. Smooth bumps on arbitrary Asplund spaces

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 19

Does every Asplund Banach space X admit a nonzero, everywhere Fréchet-differentiable function f:X→R with bounded support?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 1837: Tommaso Russo. Milan PhD manuscript, 2018; advisor Clemente Zanco; coadvisor Petr Hájek; defended 2019. Origin for question 1837: Earlier smoothness questions; thesis cites Guirao–Montesinos–Zizler (2016), Problem 144, and Hájek–Johanis (2014). Setup for question 1837: Spaces are real. Asplund means every separable closed subspace has separable dual. Cᵏ smoothness means continuous Fréchet derivatives through order k; norms are smooth away from zero.

**Source.** Tommaso Russo. *On some open problems in Banach space theory*. 2018. [primary source](https://www.uibk.ac.at/media/filer_public/80/19/8019b728-2aa6-4680-9bc8-e41a96c3d44b/phd_thesis.pdf) Location: §1.1.1, p. 3, after Lemma 1.1.5

**Literature check.** Status for question 1837, checked 6 October 2026: no full resolution found in targeted searches or the author’s later publications.

<a id="q1838"></a>

## Q1838. Godefroy’s question for continuous-function spaces

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 5.1) · **Collection** 19

For scattered compact Hausdorff K, does existence of a norming M-basis in C(K) force K to be Eberlein?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1838, 1839, 1840, 1841: Godefroy’s C(K) variant; bidual: Kalenda (2000), Question 6.14; remaining questions: Hájek–Russo. Setup for questions 1838, 1839, 1840, 1841: Spaces are real. A norming M-basis (eᵢ,fᵢ) is biorthogonal, span{eᵢ} is dense, and span{fᵢ} norms X up to a positive constant. Plichko means there are linearly dense Δ⊆X and norming D⊆X\* with {x∈Δ:f(x)≠0} countable for every f∈D. Scattered means each nonempty subset has an isolated point; Eberlein means homeomorphic to a weakly compact subset of a Banach space.

**Source.** Petr Hájek; Tommaso Russo. *Norming Markushevich bases: recent results and open problems*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.18442) Location: Problem 5.1, p. 19

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1838, 1839, 1840, 1841, checked 6 October 2026: retained in v3; no later answer found.

<a id="q1842"></a>

## Q1842. Packing-covering constant of a mixed sum

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 7.1) · **Collection** 19

Determine γ(ℓ₁⊕₂R). Is it equal to γ\*(ℓ₁⊕₂R)?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1842, 1843, 1844, 1845: Authors’ Problems 7.1, 7.3, 7.4 and 7.5. Setup for questions 1842, 1843, 1844, 1845: For real normed X, γ(X)=inf{r>0: some P⊆X is 2-separated and X=⋃\_{p∈P}B(p,r)}. Define γ\*(X) by requiring P to be an additive subgroup. Balls are closed. C(K) has supremum norm; ||(x,t)||\_{ℓ₁⊕₂R}=(||x||₁²+|t|²)½.

**Source.** Carlo Alberto De Bernardi; Tommaso Russo; Şeyda Sezgek; Jacopo Somaglia. *Packings in classical Banach spaces*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.12934) Location: Problem 7.1, p. 37

<a id="q1846"></a>

## Q1846. One convex body tiling separable Hilbert space

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 6.1) · **Collection** 19

Can ℓ₂ be tiled by translates of a single bounded convex body?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1846, 1847, 1848: Separable tiling: authors’ Problem 6.1. Disjoint tiling and ball covering continue Klee (1981), Questions 3.4 and 2.6. Setup for questions 1846, 1847, 1848: All spaces are real; c=2^{ℵ₀}. A tiling covers the space with closed sets having nonempty, pairwise disjoint interiors. A convex body is closed and convex with nonempty interior. Disjoint tilings require the entire tiles, including boundaries, to be pairwise disjoint. Point-finite means each point lies in finitely many members.

**Source.** Carlo Alberto De Bernardi; Tommaso Russo; Jacopo Somaglia. *Lattice tilings of Hilbert spaces*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04267) Location: Problem 6.1, p. 23

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1846, 1847, 1848, checked 6 October 2026: no solution found; April’s ℓ₁ result and September’s polyhedral result leave these Hilbert targets unaffected.

## Q1849. Strict convexity invariant under one isometry

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 7.1) · **Collection** 19

Do there exist a separable Banach space X and a surjective linear isometry T:X→X such that no strictly convex equivalent norm is T-invariant?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1849, 1850, 1851: Doucha’s Questions 7.1, 7.3–7.4, following de la Salle’s 2018 question. Setup for questions 1849, 1850, 1851: Spaces are real. Renormings are equivalent. For countable discrete G, Act_G(X)=Hom(G,Iso(X)) has pointwise strong-operator topology; Act_G⁺(X) restricts to positive linear isometries. Let R_G(X) denote actions admitting an invariant strictly convex renorming. L₁[0,1] uses Lebesgue measure; C(2^N) uses supremum norm.

**Source.** Michal Doucha. *Invariant strictly convex renormings*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.16182) Location: Question 7.1, p. 23

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1849, 1850, 1851, checked 6 October 2026: 2026 journal record and subsequent author list checked; no resolution found.

## Q1935. Transitivity inherited by free-space linearization

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 20

If M is a non-singleton separable complete pointed metric space and f:M→M is basepoint-preserving, Lipschitz and topologically transitive, must T_f:F(M)→F(M) be topologically transitive?

**Context.** Origin for question 1935: Abbar–Coine–Petitjean; reaffirmed by Bargetz–Kügler. Setup for question 1935: For pointed M, F(M)=closure span{δ\_x:x∈M}⊂Lip₀(M)\* over R, where Lip₀(M) has the Lipschitz norm. Set T_fδ\_x=δ\_{f(x)}. Transitivity means every pair of nonempty open sets U,V satisfies fⁿ(U)∩V≠∅ for some n≥1.

**Source.** Christian Bargetz; Leon Kügler. *Some observations on transitivity of Lipschitz operators*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07921) Location: Introduction, pp. 1–2

**Literature check.** Status for question 1935, checked 6 October 2026: September 2026 source explicitly leaves inheritance open; no later solution found by 6 October 2026.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.07921v1)


<a id="q1939"></a>

## Q1939. Projective tensor stability of DP

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 20

If X and Y have DP, must X⊗̂πY have DP?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1939, 1940: Werner’s 2001 survey, §6 Question(3). Setup for questions 1939, 1940: Spaces are real and nonzero. DP means ||I+T||=1+||T|| for every bounded rank-one T. X⊗̂πY and X⊗̂εY are completions with norms π(u)=inf Σ||x_i||||y_i|| and ε(u)=sup_{x\*∈B_X\*,y\*∈B_Y\*}|Σx\*(x_i)y\*(y_i)|, over representations u=Σx_i⊗y_i.

**Source.** Abraham Rueda Zoca. *Weak operator Daugavet property and weakly open sets in tensor product spaces*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s13163-025-00531-x) Location: Introduction; §2.2

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1939, 1940, checked 6 October 2026: 2026 journal source retains both questions; no full solution found by 6 October 2026.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.06143)

<a id="q1941"></a>

## Q1941. Super ADP without Daugavet points

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.5) · **Collection** 20

Does an infinite-dimensional super ADP space with no Daugavet points exist?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1941, 1942: Authors’ Questions 6.5 and 6.6. Setup for questions 1941, 1942: For real or complex X, super ADP means: for x∈S_X, ε>0 and relatively weakly open W⊂B_X meeting S_X, some y∈W and |θ|=1 satisfy ||x+θy||>2−ε. A Daugavet point x∈S_X satisfies sup_{y∈S}||x+y||=2 for every slice S of B_X. A slice is a nonempty open-halfspace intersection.

**Source.** Johann Langemets; Marcus Lõo; Miguel Martín; Yoël Perreau; Abraham Rueda Zoca. *The super Alternative Daugavet property for Banach spaces*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1017/S0013091525101168) Location: Question 6.5, p. 497

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1941, 1942, checked 6 October 2026: July sequel excludes K-unconditional bases for K<3/2; these questions remain unaddressed.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07631) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12880)

**Context.** Origin for questions 1941, 1942: Authors’ Questions 6.5 and 6.6. Setup for questions 1941, 1942: For real or complex X, super ADP means: for x∈S_X, ε>0 and relatively weakly open W⊂B_X meeting S_X, some y∈W and |θ|=1 satisfy ||x+θy||>2−ε. A Daugavet point x∈S_X satisfies sup_{y∈S}||x+y||=2 for every slice S of B_X. A slice is a nonempty open-halfspace intersection.

**Source.** Johann Langemets; Marcus Lõo; Miguel Martín; Yoël Perreau; Abraham Rueda Zoca. *The super Alternative Daugavet property for Banach spaces*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1017/S0013091525101168) Location: Question 6.6, p. 497

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1941, 1942, checked 6 October 2026: July sequel excludes K-unconditional bases for K<3/2; these questions remain unaddressed.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07631) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12880)

## Q1943. No norm-attaining planes after renorming

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 20

Can every nonreflexive Banach space X be equivalently renormed so that NA(X) contains no two-dimensional linear subspace?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1943, 1944: Renorming question extends Read–Rmoutil; E∖SE question originates circa 2009. Setup for questions 1943, 1944: NA(X) comprises continuous linear functionals attaining their norm on B_X. Let c=2^{ℵ₀}. E comprises f:R→R surjective on every nonempty open interval; SE⊂E requires each value to have c preimages in each such interval. κ-lineable means containing, with zero adjoined, a vector subspace of dimension κ.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.4.3) · **Collection** 21

For each Y and Γ,δ,η>0, does some C=C(Y,Γ,δ,η) make N≥Cn sufficient for every T:Y_N→Y_N, ||T||≤Γ, to admit A:Y_N→Y_n, B:Y_n→Y_N and c∈R with AB=I, ||A||||B||≤1, ||ATB−cI||≤3ηδ and |c|≤||T||, additionally c≥(1−η)δ whenever ⟨h_I,Th_I⟩≥δ|I| for all |I|≥2^(−N)?

Origin for question 2036: Thesis question, also in the author’s 2024 preprint/2025 paper. Setup for question 2036: Over R, h_I=1_{I_left}−1_{I_right} for dyadic I⊂[0,1). Let X complete dyadic step functions under a norm with ||1||\_X=1 and ||f||\_X=||g||\_X whenever |f|,|g| are equidistributed. Y completes span{h_I} under ||Σa_Ih_I||\_X or ||E|Σε\_Ia_Ih_I|||\_X, with independent random signs. Put Y_n=span{h_I:|I|≥2^(−n)} for integers n≥0; ⟨·,·⟩ denotes the integral pairing.

**Source.** Thomas Speckhofer. *Factorization of Operators on Banach Spaces*. 2025. [primary source](https://epub.jku.at/obvulihs/download/pdf/11625069) Location: Question 4.4.3; Proposition 4.4.1, pp. 78–81

## Q2039. Primariness from primary factorization

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 7.2) · **Collection** 21

Does PFP force X to be primary?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2039, 2040, 2041: Acuaviva’s Lancaster doctoral research; supervisor N. J. Laustsen (acknowledgments). Origin for questions 2039, 2040, 2041: Authors’ Questions 7.2, 7.4 and 7.5. Setup for questions 2039, 2040, 2041: For real or complex X, PFP means: every T∈B(X) admits U,V∈B(X) with UTV=I or U(I−T)V=I. UPFP adds a constant C independent of T with ||U||||V||≤C. X is primary if every bounded projection P has ran P≅X or ker P≅X.

**Source.** Antonio Acuaviva; Tomasz Kania. *Primariness and the Primary Factorisation Property*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.21711) Location: Question 7.2, p. 65

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2039, 2040, 2041, checked 6 October 2026: July sequel leaves Read’s PFP unproved; no resolution of these questions found.

**Context.** Origin for question 2138: ZFC existence question left after Talagrand 1984 CH construction. Setup for question 2138: A Boolean algebra B is Grothendieck if every weak\*-convergent sequence in C(St(B))\* is weakly convergent. It is Nikodym if every pointwise-null sequence of bounded finitely additive signed measures on B is uniformly bounded in total variation. St(B) is its Stone space.

**Source.** Damian Głodkowski; Agnieszka Widz. *Epic math battle of history: Grothendieck vs Nikodym*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.13145) Location: Question 1.4, p. 2

<a id="q2139"></a>

## Q2139. Continuum extensibility forces CH

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.1) · **Collection** 22

Does ZFC prove that c-extensibility of X implies c=ℵ₁?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2139, 2140, 2141: Authors’ remaining extension questions. Setup for questions 2139, 2140, 2141: Let X=ℓ∞/c₀ over R and c=2^{ℵ₀}. X is κ-extensible if every bounded linear T:Y→X, for closed Y⊂X with dens Y<κ, extends boundedly to X. Y-extensible restricts domains to copies of Y. Let a be the least size of an infinite maximal almost-disjoint family on N; b the least size of an eventually-unbounded family in N^N. ℓ∞^c(κ) consists of countably supported bounded functions.

**Source.** Piotr Koszmider; Małgorzata Rojek. *Almost disjoint families and some automorphic and injective properties of ℓ∞/c₀*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.22376) Location: Question 6.1(2), p. 18

## Q2234. Metric approximation over finite-dimensional subsets

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 1.1.2) · **Collection** 23

For every integer N≥1, every norm on R^N and every closed M⊂R^N with its induced metric, must F(M) have MAP?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2234: Filip Talimdjioski. PhD, University College Dublin, School of Mathematics and Statistics, 2024. Advisor: Richard J. Smith. Origin for question 2234: Godefroy 2015, A survey on Lipschitz-free Banach spaces, Problem 6.4. Setup for question 2234: For pointed real metric M, Lip₀(M) has the Lipschitz norm and F(M)=cl span{δ\_x:x∈M}⊂Lip₀(M)\*, where δ\_x(f)=f(x). Isomorphisms are bounded linear with bounded inverse. MAP means that finite-rank linear contractions approximate the identity uniformly on compact sets.

**Source.** Filip Talimdjioski. *Lipschitz-free spaces and approximation properties*. 2024. [primary source](https://researchrepository.ucd.ie/server/api/core/bitstreams/34c6b660-d86a-47cc-894b-82530cc0cce4/content) Location: Problem 1.1.2, p. 6

<a id="q2236"></a>

## Q2236. Three-Schur bound for unrectifiable bases

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.2) · **Collection** 23

Is F(M) 3-Schur for every complete purely1-unrectifiable metric space M?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2235, 2236: BAP: Kalton 2004, p. 185. Quantitative question: these authors. Setup for questions 2235, 2236: For pointed real metric M, Lip₀(M) has the Lipschitz norm and F(M)=cl span{δ\_x:x∈M}⊂Lip₀(M)\*, where δ\_x(f)=f(x). Isomorphisms are bounded linear with bounded inverse. For bounded sequences, ca(x_n)=inf_N sup_{k,l≥N}||x_k−x_l|| and δ(x_n)=sup_{x\*∈B_X\*}ca(x\*(x_n)). X is 3-Schur if ca≤3δ. Purely1-unrectifiable means every Lipschitz image of a subset of R has zero H¹-measure. BAP uses uniformly bounded finite-rank operators converging uniformly on compact sets.

**Source.** Marek Cúth; Ondřej F. K. Kalenda. *Lipschitz-free spaces over uniformly discrete metric spaces are 3-Schur*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.01875) Location: Question 1.2, p. 3

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2235, 2236, checked 6 October 2026: bounded-negative searches found no matching resolution.


<a id="q2242"></a>

## Q2242. Universal compact quotient source for finite Nagata dimension

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 23

Does a compact metric U exist such that every compact metric M of finite Nagata dimension is a Lipschitz quotient of U?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2242, 2243: Authors’ restricted-universality questions. Setup for questions 2242, 2243: A Lipschitz quotient f:U→M is onto and, for some C≥1, satisfies B_M(f(x),r/C)⊂f(B_U(x,r))⊂B_M(f(x),Cr) for every x,r>0; C may depend on M. Finite Nagata dimension means some n,c give, for every s>0, a cover by diameter≤cs sets with each diameter≤s subset meeting at most n+1 members. Pure1-unrectifiability means no bi-Lipschitz copy of a positive-Lebesgue-measure subset of R.

**Source.** Jaan Kristjan Kaasik; Andrés Quilis. *Universality of Lipschitz quotients and the curve-flat index*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.20177) Location: §7,Question 1, p. 29

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2242, 2243, checked 6 October 2026: bounded-negative searches found no matching resolution.

<a id="q2248"></a>

## Q2248. Metric approximation for proper p-free spaces

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.1) · **Collection** 23

For each 0&lt;p<1 and countable proper metric K, does F_p(K) have MAP?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2248, 2249, 2250: Authors’ Questions 6.1,6.2,6.4. Setup for questions 2248, 2249, 2250: A real p-Banach space has a complete quasinorm with ||x+y||^p≤||x||^p+||y||^p. A p-metric d has d^p satisfying the triangle inequality. F_p(M) is generated by an isometric δ:M→F_p(M), δ(0)=0, such that every basepoint-preserving Lipschitz map into a p-Banach space linearizes uniquely with equal norm. AP means finite-rank bounded operators approximate the identity uniformly on compact sets; MAP requires contractions. Proper means closed bounded sets are compact. ℓ\_p(X) is the countable p-sum.

**Source.** Fernando Albiac; José L. Ansorena; Marek Cúth; Michal Doucha. *Lipschitz free spaces isomorphic to their infinite sums and geometric applications*. 2021. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2005.06555) Location: Question 6.1, p. 36

<a id="q2334"></a>

## Q2334. Finite backward histories under hypercyclic composition

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 4.3) · **Collection** 24

If λC_φ is hypercyclic and |λ|≥1, must {n≥1:φ^{-n}({v})≠∅} be finite for every v∈T?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2334, 2335, 2336: Author’s Problems 4.3, 4.5, 4.6. Setup for questions 2334, 2335, 2336: T is an infinite locally finite rooted tree, with no degree-one vertices except possibly its root o. Write v⁻ for the parent and |v| for distance to o. L₀(T)={f:T→C:f(v)−f(v⁻)→0 as |v|→∞}, with norm max{|f(o)|,sup_{v≠o}|f(v)−f(v⁻)|}. Here φ:T→T, λ∈C, and C_φf=f∘φ is bounded. Hypercyclic means admitting a dense orbit; a hypercyclic subspace is closed, infinite-dimensional, with every nonzero vector hypercyclic.

**Source.** Antoni López-Martínez. *Frequently hypercyclic composition operators on the little Lipschitz space of a rooted tree*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.02397) Location: Problem 4.3

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2334, 2335, 2336, checked 6 October 2026: targeted searches found no matching resolution; bounded-negative evidence.

## Q2440. Equality of real Lipschitz and linear numerical indices

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 25 · *provisional entry*

Must n_L(X)=n(X) for every real Banach space X?

**Context.** Origin for question 2440: Wang–Huang–Tan, 2014; the real-space question is retained here. Setup for question 2440: For a real Banach X and Lipschitz F:X→X with F(0)=0, let v_L(F)=sup{|x\*(F(x)−F(y))|/||x−y||:x≠y, ||x\*||=1, x\*(x−y)=||x−y||}. Define n_L(X)=inf_{Lip(F)=1}v_L(F), and n(X) by restricting this infimum to bounded linear F.

**Source.** Antonio Pérez-Hernández. *Lipschitz vs Linear Numerical Index in Certain Banach Spaces*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00009-025-03044-5) Location: Introduction, Real case, pp. 3–4

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.1) · **Collection** 26

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2537: see question 2533. Origin for question 2537: Authors’ Question 1.1; found through Scherer’s doctoral genealogy. ## Q2645. Uniform tails for calibrated hypercyclic shifts

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 27

Does ρ-frequent hypercyclicity imply, for every i≥0, lim_{J→∞}sup_{k≥1}Σ\_{j≥J}α\_{i+u_{j+k}−u_k}^p=0?

**Context.** Origin for question 2645: Question following (4.3), p. 9. Setup for question 2645: Let 1≤p<∞ and B_we_0=0, B_we_n=w_ne_{n−1} on ℓ^p(N_0), with bounded positive weights; α\_0=1, α\_n=(w_1⋯w_n)^{−1}. Let ρ:[0,∞)→[0,∞) be strictly increasing, unbounded, concave and 1-Lipschitz, with ρ(0)=0; put u_k=⌊ρ^{−1}(k)⌋. Call B_w ρ-frequently hypercyclic when some x has liminf_{N→∞}#{0≤n≤N:B_w^nx∈U}/ρ(N)>0 for every nonempty open U.

**Source.** Tom Carroll and Clifford Gilmore. *ρ-Frequently Hypercyclic Operators*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.10943v1) Location: §4, p. 9; condition (4.1), p. 8

**Literature check.** Status for question 2645, checked 7 October 2026: Necessity is proved when all w_n≥1. No exact resolution located in the bounded 7 October 2026 search.

## Q2737. Odd-dimensional real Hilbert operator domains

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 3) · **Collection** 28

For every odd-dimensional real Hilbert H with dim H>1, every real Banach Y and every norm-one G:L(H)→Y, is n_G=0?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2737, 2738, 2739: Thesis Problems 3,5,11, pp. 192,196. Setup for questions 2737, 2738, 2739: Write L(X,Y) for bounded operators with the operator norm. For ||G||=1, n_G is the largest k≥0 such that max_{|ω|=1}||G+ωT||≥1+k||T|| for every T∈L(X,Y), with scalars in the field of X,Y. Let Gen(X,Y) comprise norm-one G satisfying cl conv{x∈B_X:||Gx||>1−δ}=B_X for every δ>0. Closures are norm closures.

**Source.** Alicia Quero de la Rosa. *On the geometry around an operator between Banach spaces*. 2023. Advisor(s): Miguel Martín Suárez and Francisco Javier Merí de la Maza. ## Q2740. Transfinite square renormings

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 2.2) · **Collection** 28

For every infinite cardinal κ, does every X containing an isomorphic copy of c₀(κ) admit an equivalent SQ_<κ norm?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2740, 2741, 2742: Thesis Problems 2.2,2.3,3.1,3.2, pp. 46,58. Setup for questions 2740, 2741, 2742: All spaces are real. SQ_<κ means every A⊂S_X with |A|<κ admits y∈S_X with ||x±y||≤1 for all x∈A. LASQ means every x∈S_X admits (y_n)⊂S_X with ||x±y_n||→1. SSD2P means each finite family of slices S_i of B_X and ε>0 admits x_i∈S_i and y∈B_X with ||y||>1−ε and x_i±y∈S_i. A slice is B_X∩{f>1−α}, f∈S_{X\*},α>0.

**Source.** Stefano Ciaci. *Transfinite geometric properties of the unit ball in Banach spaces*. 2024. Advisor(s): Johann Langemets and Aleksei Lissitsin. If X is LASQ, does every x∈S_X admit a weakly null net (y_α)⊂S_X with ||x±y_α||→1?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2740, 2741, 2742: Thesis Problems 2.2,2.3,3.1,3.2, pp. 46,58. Setup for questions 2740, 2741, 2742: All spaces are real. SQ_<κ means every A⊂S_X with |A|<κ admits y∈S_X with ||x±y||≤1 for all x∈A. LASQ means every x∈S_X admits (y_n)⊂S_X with ||x±y_n||→1. SSD2P means each finite family of slices S_i of B_X and ε>0 admits x_i∈S_i and y∈B_X with ||y||>1−ε and x_i±y∈S_i. A slice is B_X∩{f>1−α}, f∈S_{X\*},α>0.

**Source.** Stefano Ciaci. *Transfinite geometric properties of the unit ball in Banach spaces*. 2024. Advisor(s): Johann Langemets and Aleksei Lissitsin. [primary source](https://dspace.ut.ee/server/api/core/bitstreams/97f34bef-7753-42db-83f4-0fcc87b29ff1/content) Location: Problem 2.3, p. 46

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2740, 2741, 2742, checked 7 October 2026: No exact resolution found in a bounded literature check on 7 October 2026.

<a id="q2743"></a>

## Q2743. Chaotic free operators

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5.1) · **Collection** 28

Can T_f be chaotic without being frequently hypercyclic?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2743, 2744, 2745: Thesis Question 5.1; Cobollo–Hájek (2025); Abbar–Coine–Petitjean’s question reaffirmed by Cobollo–Ernst–Menet–Peris (2026). Setup for questions 2743, 2744, 2745: For pointed metric M, let Lip₀(M) comprise real Lipschitz functions vanishing at 0, with Lipschitz norm; F(M)=cl span{δ\_x:x∈M}⊂Lip₀(M)\*. Lipschitz f fixing 0 induces T_fδ\_x=δ\_{f(x)}. Hypercyclic means dense orbit; chaotic also requires dense periodic vectors; frequent hypercyclicity means positive-lower-density visits to each nonempty open set. Octahedrality means ∀ finite-dimensional E, ε>0 ∃y∈S_X: ||x+ty||≥(1−ε)(||x||+|t|), for x∈E,t∈R.

**Source.** Christian Cobollo Gómez. *Geometric Structures in Banach Spaces: Differentiability, Operators and their Dynamics*. 2025. Advisor(s): Antonio José Guirao Sánchez, Vicente Montesinos Santalucía, Alfred Peris Manguillot. [primary source](https://riunet.upv.es/server/api/core/bitstreams/e5608006-65bf-48d5-a31c-a0595dd95708/content) Location: Thesis Question 5.1, p. 167

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2743, 2744, 2745, checked 7 October 2026: Weak mixing remains open in September 2026; complemented-ℓ₁ smoothness is settled. No general resolution found by 7 October 2026.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.17874v1) · [2](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jmaa.2024.128968)

## Q2746. Strong diameter-two dual renormings

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 7) · **Collection** 28

Does every real Banach X containing an isomorphic copy of ℓ₁ admit an equivalent norm whose induced dual norm makes X\* have SD2P?

**Context.** Origin for question 2746: Thesis Question 7, p. 44; Godefroy’s bidual-octahedral renorming problem. Setup for question 2746: SD2P means every finite convex combination of slices of the closed unit ball has diameter 2; slices use all continuous linear functionals, not merely a predual.

## Q2747. Independence of Hardy exponent for Volterra boundedness

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 28

Is T_g bounded on H² if and only if it is bounded on H^p for every 0&lt;p<∞?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2747: see question 2735. Origin for question 2747: Question 1, printed p. 2 / PDF p. 4. Setup for question 2747: For 0&lt;p<∞, H^p is the completion of Dirichlet polynomials for ||f||\_p^p=lim_{T→∞}(2T)^{−1}∫\_{−T}^T|f(it)|^pdt. For a Dirichlet series g holomorphic on Re s>1/2, set T_gf(s)=−∫\_s^{+∞}f(w)g′(w)dw along the horizontal ray.

**Source.** Ole Fredrik Brevig, Karl-Mikael Perfekt and Kristian Seip. *Volterra operators on Hardy spaces of Dirichlet series*. 2019. [primary source](https://centaur.reading.ac.uk/71297/1/volterra_final4.pdf) Location: Question 1, printed p. 2 / PDF p. 4

## Q2749. Isometric ideals under injective tensor products

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4) · **Collection** 28

If Y is an isometric ideal in X and Z is any real Banach space, must Y⊗̂\_εZ be an isometric ideal in X⊗̂\_εZ?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2749: see question 2746. Origin for question 2749: Question 4, §7; Definition 4.1. Setup for question 2749: For real Banach Y⊂X, call Y an isometric ideal if every finite-dimensional E⊂X admits a linear isometry T:E→Y with T(e)=e for e∈E∩Y.

**Source.** Esteban Martínez Vañó and Abraham Rueda Zoca. *Transfinite (almost isometric) ideals in Banach spaces*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s13163-026-00572-w) Location: Question 4, §7; arXiv p. 36

## Q2750. Numerical-radius approximation forces property-nu

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.4) · **Collection** 28

Does every real or complex Banach space with BPBp-nu have property(nu)?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2750: see question 2748. Origin for question 2750: Question 4.4, p. 18; Definitions 1.4 and 4.1. Setup for question 2750: Let Π(X)={(x,x\*):||x||=||x\*||=x\*(x)=1}, ν(T)=sup_{Π(X)}|x\*(Tx)|. BPBp-nu means ∀ε>0 ∃η>0: ν(T)=1, (x,x\*)∈Π(X), |x\*(Tx)|>1−η imply S,(y,y\*)∈Π(X) with ν(S)=|y\*(Sy)|=1 and max{||S−T||,||y−x||,||y\*−x\*||}<ε. Property(nu) uses the same closeness conclusion but starts from ||T||=||x||=||x\*||=|x\*(Tx)|=1, Re x\*(x)>1−η and requires ||S||=y\*(y)=|y\*(Sy)|=1.

**Source.** Sun Kwang Kim, Han Ju Lee, Miguel Martín and Óscar Roldán. *The Bishop–Phelps–Bollobás property for the numerical radius: a Zizler-type approach*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.06625v1) Location: Question 4.4, p. 18

**Literature check.** Status for question 2750, checked 7 October 2026: Proved when n(X)=1; arbitrary numerical index remains open in April 2026. No exact resolution found in a bounded literature check on 7 October 2026.

**Further links.** [1](https://producciocientifica.uv.es/documentos/6531007717325e4eea3441ea)


<a id="q2835"></a>

## Q2835. Smooth supercyclicity and mixing

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 29

Must every supercyclic T in this class be mixing?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2835: Christopher Foster. Supercyclicity and Disjoint Dynamics of Weighted Composition Operators. PhD, Bowling Green State University, 2024. Advisors: Juan Bès. Origin for question 2835: Thesis p. 20; journal§ 5 Question 1, also arXiv§ 5 p. 16. Setup for question 2835: Let d≥2, Ω⊂R^d be open, K∈{R,C}, and ω:Ω→K, ψ:Ω→Ω be smooth. On C∞(Ω,K), with compact-uniform convergence of every derivative, define Tf=ω(f∘ψ). Supercyclicity means some {λTⁿf:λ∈K,n≥0} is dense; mixing means TⁿU∩V≠∅ for every sufficiently large n, for every pair of nonempty open sets U,V.

**Source.** Juan Bès; Christopher Foster. *Supercyclic weighted composition operators on the space of smooth functions*. 2025. [primary source](https://www.mdpi.com/2227-7390/13/18/2944) Location: Journal §5, Question 1,p. 18; arXiv p. 16

**Literature check.** Status for question 2835, checked 7 October 2026: The September 2025 journal retains Question 1; no later resolution found in bounded searches through 7 October 2026.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.16425v1) · [2](https://etd.ohiolink.edu/acprod/odb_etd/ws/send_file/send?accession=bgsu171944597837949&disposition=inline)

## Q2836. Contracting punctured-domain symbols

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 29

For every such Ω,a and entire F, is T_F non-supercyclic?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2836: see question 2835. Origin for question 2836: Thesis Remark 3.6.5,p. 44; paper Remark 4.8,p. 18. Setup for question 2836: Let Ω⊂C be a simply connected domain containing0, Ω\*=Ω∖{0}, 0<|a|<1 and aΩ⊆Ω. Equip H(Ω\*) with compact-uniform convergence and define T_Ff(z)=F(1/z)f(az) for entire F. Supercyclic means that some scalar-multiple orbit {λT_Fⁿf:λ∈C,n≥0} is dense.

**Source.** Juan Bès; Christopher Foster. *Dynamics of composition operators on spaces of holomorphic functions on plane domains*. 2024. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.05600v1) Location: Remark 4.8,p. 18

**Literature check.** Status for question 2836, checked 7 October 2026: The 2025 smooth-composition paper still identifies the punctured-disc case as exceptional; no exact resolution found through 7 October 2026.

<a id="q2837"></a>

## Q2837. Doubling-weight Berger–Coburn equivalence

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 1.5) · **Collection** 29

For every 1&lt;p<∞ and every such φ,f, does H_f∈S_p imply H_conj(f)∈S_p?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2837, 2838: Ghazaleh Asghari. Toeplitz, Hankel, and Berezin transforms on Fock-type spaces. Doctoral thesis, University of Reading, dated December 2025. Supervisor: Jani A. Virtanen. Origin for questions 2837, 2838: Published Open Problems 1.5,1.8,p. 5; reproduced in dissertation Appendix A. Setup for questions 2837, 2838: Take smooth subharmonic φ:C→R with nonzero doubling measure μ=ΔφdA: μ(B(z,2r))≤Cμ(B(z,r)) for all z,r>0. Set L²\_φ=L²(C,e^(−2φ)dA), F²\_φ=H(C)∩L²\_φ, let P_φ be its orthogonal projection, and H_f g=(I−P_φ)(fg). Here f∈L∞(C); S_p consists of compact operators whose singular values have summable pth powers.

**Source.** Ghazaleh Asghari; Zhangjian Hu; Jani A. Virtanen. *Schatten class Hankel operators on doubling Fock spaces and the Berger-Coburn phenomenon*. 2024. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jmaa.2024.128596) Location: Open Problem 1.5,p. 5

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2837, 2838, checked 7 October 2026: December 2025 thesis retains both;2026 Gaussian r-summing and Toeplitz heat-transform results concern different questions. No exact later resolution found.

**Further links.** [1](https://centaur.reading.ac.uk/116765/8/1-s2.0-S0022247X24005183-main.pdf) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.01197) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.20859) · [4](https://www.reading.ac.uk/maths-and-stats/-/media/project/uor-main/schools-departments/maths/documents/2025-asghari-ghazaleh-thesis.pdf?hash=02699A4D20EA89AD28A88366306C0B95&la=en)

<a id="q2842"></a>

## Q2842. Frequent algebras in pointwise ℓp

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5.22) · **Collection** 29

For a fixed 1≤p<∞, can B_w on pointwise ℓ^p have a frequently hypercyclic algebra?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2842, 2843, 2844: Fernando Vieira Costa Júnior. Hypercyclic vectors and algebras. PhD, Université Clermont Auvergne, 2021. Advisors: Frédéric Bayart. Origin for questions 2842, 2843, 2844: Questions 5.22,5.24,p.45;6.2,p.48, arXiv v2. Setup for questions 2842, 2843, 2844: Scalars are complex. A hypercyclic algebra for T is a nonzero subalgebra A whose nonzero elements have dense T-orbits; “frequently” additionally requires positive lower density of visits to every nonempty open set. Write (B_wx)\_n=w_(n+1)x_(n+1), B=B_1, and (x\*y)\_n=Σ\_(j=0)^n x_jy_(n−j). Weights are bounded and nonzero. H(C) has compact-uniform topology and pointwise multiplication.

**Source.** Frédéric Bayart; Fernando Costa Júnior; Dimitris Papathanasiou. *Baire theorem and hypercyclic algebras*. 2021. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.aim.2020.107419) Location: Question 5.22,p. 45

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2842, 2843, 2844, checked 7 October 2026: No exact later resolution found through 7 October 2026; the 2026 existence theorem does not prescribe these operators.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/1910.05000v2) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.13983v1) · [3](https://fcostajr.com/assets/pdf/en-us/Fernando-CV.pdf)

Origin for questions 2842, 2843, 2844: Questions 5.22,5.24,p.45;6.2,p.48, arXiv v2. Setup for questions 2842, 2843, 2844: Scalars are complex. A hypercyclic algebra for T is a nonzero subalgebra A whose nonzero elements have dense T-orbits; “frequently” additionally requires positive lower density of visits to every nonempty open set. Write (B_wx)\_n=w_(n+1)x_(n+1), B=B_1, and (x\*y)\_n=Σ\_(j=0)^n x_jy_(n−j). Weights are bounded and nonzero. H(C) has compact-uniform topology and pointwise multiplication.

**Source.** Frédéric Bayart; Fernando Costa Júnior; Dimitris Papathanasiou. *Baire theorem and hypercyclic algebras*. 2021. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.aim.2020.107419) Location: Question 6.2,p. 48

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2842, 2843, 2844, checked 7 October 2026: No exact later resolution found through 7 October 2026; the 2026 existence theorem does not prescribe these operators.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/1910.05000v2) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.13983v1) · [3](https://fcostajr.com/assets/pdf/en-us/Fernando-CV.pdf)


<a id="q2845"></a>

## Q2845. Closed algebras on arbitrary Banach algebras

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 29

Does each separable infinite-dimensional Banach algebra admit some T with a closed infinite-dimensional hypercyclic algebra?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2845, 2846: see question 2842. Origin for questions 2845, 2846: Section 4, Questions 1–2,p. 6; these are residual problems after the Fréchet-algebra existence theorem. Setup for questions 2845, 2846: Work over R or C. An F-algebra is a complete metrizable Hausdorff topological vector space with jointly continuous associative bilinear multiplication; commutativity and a unit are not assumed. A continuous norm need not induce its topology. A hypercyclic algebra for a continuous linear T is a nonzero subalgebra whose nonzero vectors have dense T-orbits.

**Source.** Fernando Costa Júnior; Álvaro Rocha. *Existence of hypercyclic algebras in Fréchet algebras*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.13983v1) Location: §4, Question 1,p. 6

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2845, 2846, checked 7 October 2026: 12 September 2026 v1 explicitly poses both; no later resolution found by 7 October 2026.

**Further links.** [1](https://fcostajr.com/assets/pdf/en-us/Fernando-CV.pdf)

<a id="q2847"></a>

## Q2847. Hereditary nonmixing

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 9.16) · **Collection** 29

Can an HFHC T fail mixing: ∃nonempty open U,V with TⁿU∩V=∅ for infinitely many n?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2847, 2848, 2849: see question 2842. Origin for questions 2847, 2848, 2849: Questions 9.16–9.18,p.38, author postprint. Setup for questions 2847, 2848, 2849: On an infinite-dimensional real/complex Polish topological vector space X, let T be continuous linear; N_T(x,U)={n≥0:Tⁿx∈U}, and d_\*,d^\* denote lower/upper natural density. HFHC means: for every sequence (A_j,U_j) with A_j⊂N, d_\*(A_j)>0 and U_j nonempty open, ∃x∀j d_\*(A_j∩N_T(x,U_j))>0. HUFHC substitutes d^\* for both occurrences of d_\*.

**Source.** Frédéric Bayart; Sophie Grivaux; Étienne Matheron; Quentin Menet. *Hereditarily frequently hypercyclic operators and disjoint frequent hypercyclicity*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1017/etds.2025.13) Location: Question 9.16,p. 38

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2847, 2848, 2849, checked 7 October 2026: Postprint retains these questions; no later resolution located.

<a id="q2850"></a>

## Q2850. Frequent hypercyclicity of the square

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5) · **Collection** 29

If T is frequently hypercyclic, must (x,y)↦(Tx,Ty) be frequently hypercyclic on X×X?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2850, 2851: Clifford Gilmore. Hypercyclicity of Derivations and Optimal Growth of Frequently Hypercyclic Harmonic Functions. PhD, University of Helsinki, 2017. Advisors: Hans-Olav Tylli. Origin for questions 2850, 2851: Questions 5–6,p. 72. Question 5 follows Bayart–Grivaux; Question 6 repeats Grivaux 2018,Question 3.2. Setup for questions 2850, 2851: Let T be a bounded linear operator on a separable infinite-dimensional Banach space X. Put N_T(x,U)={n≥0:Tⁿx∈U}; lower/upper density are liminf/limsup of |A∩{0,…,N−1}|/N. A vector x is frequently hypercyclic if every nonempty open U has positive lower-density N_T(x,U); T is frequently hypercyclic if such x exists.

**Source.** Clifford Gilmore. *Linear Dynamical Systems*. 2020. [primary source](https://www.irishmathsoc.org/bull86/pdfs/Articles/Gilmore/Gilmore.pdf) Location: Question 5,p. 72

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2850, 2851, checked 7 October 2026: The 2025 hereditary-frequency paper reaffirms the equal-direct-sum question. No exact later solution to either question found through 7 October 2026.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.07103v1) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/1710.07901v2) · [3](https://researchportal.helsinki.fi/en/publications/hypercyclicity-of-derivations-and-optimal-growth-of-frequently-hy/)

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 5.3) · **Collection** 30

For 1&lt;p<2 and p′=p/(p−1), does (Σ\_I||S_I f||\_p^p′)^(1/p′)≤C_p||f||\_p hold for every interval partition of Z and scalar trigonometric polynomial f?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2940, 2941: Chenxi Deng. Fourier Multiplier Methods in the Study of Growth and Decay of Semigroups. PhD, Delft University of Technology, 2025. ## Q2942. Zero-decay fractional endpoint

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 30

Must there be K such that ||T(t)x||≤K||x||\_(D((−A)^τ)) for all t≥1 and x∈D((−A)^τ), with the fractional-domain graph norm?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2942: see question 2940. Origin for question 2942: Final journal §1.3, p. 4; arXiv p. 3; thesis p. 24. Suppose {Re λ>0}⊂ρ(A) and ||(λ−A)⁻¹||≤C(1+|λ|)ᵝ there, with β>0. Put τ=β+1/p−1/p′, using 1/∞=0.

<a id="q2943"></a>

## Q2943. Residual smooth renormings

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 30

If X admits an equivalent norm Fréchet differentiable at every nonzero point, is the set of such equivalent norms residual?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 2943, 2944: Deepak Gothwal. Mazur’s Intersection Property and its Variants. PhD, Indian Statistical Institute, Kolkata, 2025. Advisors: Pradipta Bandyopadhyay. Origin for questions 2943, 2944: §6 Questions 1,3, p. 19; both also identified in the doctoral abstract. Setup for questions 2943, 2944: All spaces are real. Equivalent norms carry uniform convergence on the original unit ball; residual means containing a dense Gδ. UMIP means: for every ε>0,M≥2 there is K such that every closed convex C of diameter≤M and x with dist(x,C)≥ε admit a closed ball B of radius≤K containing C with dist(x,B)≥ε/2.

**Source.** Pradipta Bandyopadhyay; Deepak Gothwal. *(Asymptotic) uniform smoothness, ball separation and residuality results*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.18217v1) Location: §6, Question 1, p. 19

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2943, 2944, checked 7 October 2026: The 2025 paper explicitly leaves both open; Bounded searches through 7 October 2026 found no exact later resolution.

**Further links.** [1](https://digitalcommons.isical.ac.in/doctoral-theses/628/)

## Q2954. Fork convexity and power renorming

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 3) · **Collection** 30

If X is fork p-convex, must it admit an equivalent norm whose modulus of convexity satisfies δ\_X(ε)≥cε^p for some c>0 and all 0<ε≤2?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 2954: Christopher Gartland. BiLipschitz embeddings and nonembeddings of metric spaces and related problems. PhD, University of Illinois at Urbana-Champaign, 2020. Advisors: Jeremy Tyson. Origin for question 2954: Post-thesis paper origin: §9, Problem 3, p. 60; Definition 38, (51), p. 44. Setup for question 2954: For 2≤p<∞ and a Banach space X, consider all k≥1 and maps f:{−1,1}^{≤2^k}→X. Put A_ℓ=max_{|ε|=ℓ}||f(ε)−f(ε₁,…,ε\_(ℓ−1))|| and F_(s,t)=2^(−s)min_{|ε|=(2t−1)2^s; |δ|=|δ′|=2^s−1}||f(ε,−1,δ)−f(ε,1,δ′)||. X is fork p-convex if some Π makes Σ\_(s=1)^(k−1)2^(−k+1+s)Σ\_(t=1)^(2^(k−1−s))F_(s,t)^p≤Π^p2^(−k)Σ\_(ℓ=1)^(2^k)A_ℓ^p for every k,f.

**Source.** Florent P. Baudier; Christopher Gartland. *Umbel convexity and the geometry of trees*. 2024. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.aim.2023.109461) Location: §9, Problem 3, p. 60

**Literature check.** Status for question 2954, checked 7 October 2026: Final journal and February 2025 refereed version retain the question; no exact later resolution found.

<a id="q3040"></a>

## Q3040. Dual-ball model of every finite-dimensional horoboundary

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 31

For every finite-dimensional X, is there a homeomorphism X^h→B_{X\*} carrying ∂\_hX onto S_{X\*} and its strata bijectively onto relative interiors of proper faces?

**Context.** Origin for question 3040: Kapovich–Leeb’s finite-dimensional question, repeated by Power on pp. 10–11. Setup for question 3040: For real normed X, set h_x(y)=||y−x||−||x|| and let X^h be their pointwise closure; ∂\_hX=X^h∖{h_x:x∈X}. Boundary strata satisfy h∼k iff sup_X|h−k|<∞. Busemann points are boundary limits of nets (x_α) satisfying eventually ||x_β−x_α||+||x_α−x₀||−||x_β−x₀||<ε for β≥α, every ε>0 and some x₀∈X. B_{X\*},S_{X\*} denote the closed dual ball and sphere. Use pointwise boundary topology and weak-\* dual topology. Relative interior is interior in the affine hull.

**Source.** Kieran Power. *Geometry and topology of horofunction compactifications*. 2025. Advisor(s): Bas Lemmens and Rowena Paget. [primary source](https://kar.kent.ac.uk/111237/1/12Power2025PhDFinal.pdf) Location: Introduction pp. 10–11; concluding discussion pp. 274–275

**Literature check.** Status for question 3040, checked 8 October 2026: Thesis retains the general questions; the 2026 product paper handles special norms. No general resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://kar.kent.ac.uk/106628/1/Product%20of%20ell1%20v2.pdf) · [2](https://kar.kent.ac.uk/111237/)

## Q3135. Equality of fractional approximation entropies

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 32

For every 0<α<1, must sup_{δ>0}liminf_n n^{−α}a_n(δ)=sup_{δ>0}limsup_n n^{−α}a_n(δ)?

**Context.** Origin for questions 3135, 3136: Thesis questions after Definitions 3.2.21 and 5.1.4. Setup for questions 3135, 3136: Put O_n=⋃\_{j=0}^{n−1}T^jO. For unitary T on Hilbert H and finite O⊂H, let a_n(δ)=min{dim F: sup_{x∈O_n}dist(x,F)≤δ}, over linear subspaces F. Separately, let E be a Banach lattice with ||e||=1, e≥0 and dense principal ideal {x: |x|≤ce for some c≥0}. Let T:E→E be positive, contractive and fix e. For finite O⊂E, let b_n(δ) be the minimum rank of a positive contractive projection P fixing e and satisfying ||Px−x||≤δ for x∈O_n. Infinite rank is allowed.

**Source.** Julian Hölz. *Characterization, long-term behavior, and approximation of Koopman operators*. 2026. Advisor(s): Jochen Glück. [primary source](https://elekpub.bib.uni-wuppertal.de/ubwhs/download/pdf/8293069) Location: p. 91, after Definition 3.2.21

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3135, 3136, checked 8 October 2026: April 2026 thesis checked; no later exact resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://elekpub.bib.uni-wuppertal.de/ubwhs/content/titleinfo/8293069) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20269)

## Q3139. Maximal roughness of super-ADP norms

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.1) · **Collection** 32

Must every infinite-dimensional super-ADP space have a 2-rough norm?

**Context.** Origin for questions 3139, 3140: Final-version Questions 6.1 and 6.4. Setup for questions 3139, 3140: An infinite-dimensional real or complex Banach space X has super ADP if sup_{y∈W,|θ|=1}||x+θy||=2 for every x∈S_X and relatively weakly open W⊂B_X meeting S_X. Its norm is 2-rough if limsup_{h→0}(||x+h||+||x−h||−2)/||h||=2 for every x∈S_X.

**Source.** Johann Langemets, Marcus Lõo, Miguel Martín, Yoël Perreau and Abraham Rueda Zoca. *The super Alternative Daugavet property for Banach spaces*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1017/S0013091525101168) Location: Question 6.1, p. 496

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12880v2)

## Q3142. Möbius cancellation for rigid contractions

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 2) · **Collection** 32

For every rigid contraction on a separable Banach space E, does M_N(T)v→0 weakly for every v∈E?

**Context.** Origin for questions 3142, 3143: Section 6, Problems 2 and 3; Theorem 2.8 supplies the latter’s assumptions. Setup for questions 3142, 3143: Let μ be the Möbius function and M_N(T)v=N⁻¹Σ\_{n=1}^N μ(n)Tⁿv. Power bounded means sup_n||Tⁿ||<∞. A contraction T is rigid if T^{n_j}v→v in norm for every v along one sequence n_j→∞. Mean ergodicity means convergence in norm of the Cesàro averages.

**Source.** El Houcein El Abdalaoui and Michael Lin. *Operator ergodic theorems with Möbius “weights”*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15960v1) Location: Problem 2, p. 31

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3142, 3143, checked 8 October 2026: July 2026 v1 checked; no later exact resolution located.

A contraction T is rigid if T^{n_j}v→v in norm for every v along one sequence n_j→∞. Mean ergodicity means convergence in norm of the Cesàro averages.

## Q3144. Lower bounds for extremal polynomial zero angles

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 32

Must t_j>(2pj−p−1)π/(dp+2) for every 1≤j≤⌊d/2⌋?

**Context.** Origin for question 3144: Explicit expected zero-location inequality in Sections 5 and 7. Setup for question 3144: For integer d≥2 and 2≤p≤4, let φ\_{d,p} minimize (2π)⁻¹∫\_{−π}^π|P(e^{it})|^pdt among complex polynomials of degree≤d with P(1)=1. Its zeros are e^{±it_j}, 0&lt;t₁≤⋯≤t_{⌊d/2⌋}≤π, together with −1 when d is odd.

**Source.** Sarah May Instanes. *Point evaluation for polynomials on the circle*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s13348-026-00516-z) Location: Section 7 final paragraph; Section 5 after Theorem 14

**Literature check.** Status for question 3144, checked 8 October 2026: June 2026 final text retains the strict inequality.

## Q3145. Finite-q Levin–Lubinsky asymptotics

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 32

For every 0&lt;p&lt;q<∞, is lim_{n→∞}n^{1/q−1/p}C_{p,q}(T_n)=C_{p,q}(E₁)?

**Context.** Origin for questions 3145, 3146: Older questions re-stated: Conclusion items 1 and 7; not newly posed by the 2026 translation. Setup for questions 3145, 3146: Let T_n={Σ\_{|k|≤n}a_ke^{ikx}} and let E₁ be the entire functions of exponential type≤1. Set C_{p,q}(Y)=sup_{0≠f∈Y∩L^p}||f||\_q/||f||\_p, using unnormalized Lebesgue measure on [−π,π] for T_n and on R for E₁. For d≥1, PW₁¹(R^d) consists of integrable functions whose Fourier transform ∫f(x)e^{−ix·ξ}dx is supported in {|ξ|≤1}.

**Source.** D. V. Gorbachev. *Sharp Bernstein–Nikolskii inequalities for polynomials and entire functions of exponential type*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32072v1) Location: Conclusion item 1, p. 36; discussion after (27), p. 15

## Q3151. Second-order Nikolskii asymptotics

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1) · **Collection** 32

Does d^{1/3}(π/2−2^d L\*(d)) converge to a finite strictly positive constant as d→∞?

**Context.** Origin for question 3151: Conjecture 1, following the paper’s leading-asymptotic theorem. Setup for question 3151: Let PW₁¹(R^d) be the integrable functions whose Fourier transform ∫f(x)e^{−ix·ξ}dx is supported in the unit Euclidean ball, and let v_d be that ball’s volume. Set C_d=1/inf{||f||₁:f∈PW₁¹(R^d),f(0)=1}, and L\*(d)=(2π)^d C_d/v_d.

**Source.** D. V. Gorbachev. *Exact Asymptotics of the Multidimensional Nikolskii Constant*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06240v1) Location: Conjecture 1, p. 2

<a id="q3236"></a>

## Q3236. Exact invariance and Fatou properties of sum quasinorms

**Status:** Solved here: proved · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 33

Does the displayed sum quasinorm always depend only on the decreasing rearrangement and satisfy ||f_j||\_{A+B}↑||f||\_{A+B} whenever 0≤f_j↑f?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3236: Fine properties of certain specific function spaces. Dalimil Peša. Charles University PhD, defended 28 March 2024. Advisor: Luboš Pick. Origin for question 3236: General inheritance question, §1.2 pp. 3–4; later paper after Peša’s doctorate. Setup for question 3236: Let A,B be rearrangement-invariant quasi-Banach spaces of measurable complex functions on a σ-finite resonant measure space: nonatomic, or atomic with equal-mass atoms. Their quasinorms depend only on decreasing rearrangements, are lattice monotone, finite on indicators of finite-measure sets, and satisfy ||f_j||↑||f|| when 0≤f_j↑f. Set ||f||\_{A+B}=inf_{f=a+b}(||a||\_A+||b||\_B).

**Source.** Dalimil Peša. *On the sums of rearrangement-invariant quasi-Banach function spaces and their relationship to amalgams*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.10825v2) Location: §1.2 p. 4; Corollary 3.5 p. 12

**Literature check.** Status for question 3236, checked 8 October 2026: No exact later resolution located; checked 8 October 2026.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.25419) · [2](https://dspace.cuni.cz/handle/20.500.11956/188935)

**Result (2026-10-09).** The actual infimum sum quasinorm of the source rearrangement-invariant Fatou quasi-Banach function spaces is exactly rearrangement invariant and has the exact Fatou property on every sigma-finite resonant measure space. Full source scope, including infinite measure and equal-mass atomic spaces. Attainment is not needed; an auxiliary example shows it can fail on a nonatomic space.

[Proof](../../solutions/round-2026-10-09/algebra/Q3236-proof.md) · [Internal review](../../solutions/round-2026-10-09/reviews/root-review.md) · [Exact computational controls](../../solutions/round-2026-10-09/algebra/verification.json). This is a research proposal with a separate internal AI review, not an externally peer-reviewed result.



<a id="q3238"></a>

## Q3238. Isometric semi-greediness versus almost-greediness

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4) · **Collection** 33

Are 1-semi-greediness and 1-almost-greediness equivalent?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3238: Thresholding Greedy Algorithms in Banach spaces. Pablo Manuel Berná Larrosa. Universidad Autónoma de Madrid PhD, 29 July 2019. Advisors: Gustavo A. Garrigós Aniorte and Eugenio Hernández Rodríguez. Origin for question 3238: Banach case: Berná’s 2019 thesis§ 5.4Question2; quasi-Banach extension repeated as García’sQuestion4. Setup for question 3238: Let (e_n,e_n\*)⊂X×X\* be biorthogonal in a real or complex quasi-Banach X, with span{e_n} dense, span{e_n\*} weak\*-dense in X\*, and both ||e_n|| and ||e_n\*|| uniformly bounded above and away from zero. write P_Ax=Σ\_{n∈A}e_n\*(x)e_n and σ\_m(x)=inf_{|B|≤m, a_n}||x−Σ\_{n∈B}a_ne_n||. A finite A is greedy for x if min_{n∈A}|e_n\*(x)|≥sup_{n∉A}|e_n\*(x)|. The basis is 1-semi-greedy if dist(x,span{e_n:n∈A})≤σ\_|A|(x), and 1-almost-greedy if ||x−P_Ax||≤inf_{|B|≤|A|}||x−P_Bx||, for all x,A.

**Source.** Andrea García. *Greedy algorithms: a review and open problems*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1186/s13660-025-03254-1) Location: Question 4,p. 20 of 22; thesis§ 5.4Question2,p. 112

**Literature check.** Status for question 3238, checked 8 October 2026: Final Question 4 retained; October counterexample concerns semi-greediness with larger constants.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1515/dema-2024-0047) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05052) · [3](https://webs.um.es/gustavo.garrigos/TF.html)

## Q3239. Do bounded greedy de la Vallée-Poussin means force quasi-greediness?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 33

If a constant C bounds ||V_mx||≤C||x|| for all x,m and all greedy orderings, must a constant K bound ||G_mx||≤K||x|| for all these choices?

**Context.** Origin for question 3239: Question 1; paper-origin. Setup for question 3239: Let (e_n,e_n\*) be a biorthogonal system with dense span in a real or complex Banach X and sup_n||e_n||,sup_n||e_n\*||<∞. For each greedy ordering (k_j) of x, put G_mx=Σ\_{j≤m}e_{k_j}\*(x)e_{k_j} and C_mx=m⁻¹Σ\_{j≤m}G_jx. Put V_mx=2C_{2m}x−C_mx. Here k_j are distinct and min_{j≤m}|e_{k_j}\*(x)|≥sup_{k∉{k₁,…,k_m}}|e_k\*(x)| for every m.

**Source.** Miguel Berasategui; Pablo M. Berná; Stephen J. Dilworth; Denka Kutzarova. *Summability Methods for the Greedy Algorithm in Banach spaces*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.13350v2) Location: §7,Question 1,p. 32

**Literature check.** Status for question 3239, checked 8 October 2026: No exact later resolution located; checked 8 October 2026.

## Q3240. Invariant subspaces for arbitrary free self-map linearizations

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 2) · **Collection** 33

Must T have a closed linear subspace S with {0}⊊S⊊F(M,e) and T(S)⊂S?

**Context.** Origin for question 3240: Question 2; its basepoint-preserving special case is not separately counted. Setup for question 3240: Let M be an infinite complete separable metric space, e∈M, and K=R or C. Let Lip₀(M,e) be K-valued Lipschitz functions vanishing at e, with Lipschitz norm. In its dual put δ\_x(φ)=φ(x), F(M,e)=closure span{δ\_x:x∈M}. For Lipschitz f:M→M define Tδ\_x=δ\_{f(x)}−δ\_{f(e)}.

**Source.** Clément Coine; Pedro L. Kaufmann; Colin Petitjean; Sebastián Tapia-García. *Invariant subspaces for free linearizations of Lipschitz maps*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02213v3) Location: Question 2,p. 4; standing assumptionsp.6

## Q3241. Free subgroups forced by paradoxical towers

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.1.5) · **Collection** 33

Must every group with paradoxical towers contain a subgroup isomorphic to the free group F₂?

**Context.** Origin for question 3241: Question 6.1.5, following Gardella–Geffen–Kranz–Naryshkin’s definition. Setup for question 3241: A discrete group G has paradoxical towers if some integer n≥1 has the following property: for every finite D⊂G there are A₁,…,A_n⊂G and g₁,…,g_n∈G such that G=⋃\_{j=1}^n g_jA_j, while all sets dA_j, indexed by (d,j)∈D×{1,…,n}, are pairwise disjoint.

**Source.** Eusebio Gardella. *Classifiability of crossed products*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06586v1) Location: Definition 5.2.1, p. 44; Question 6.1.5, p. 47

**Literature check.** Status for question 3241, checked 8 October 2026: October 5 author version retains Question 6.1.5; no later exact resolution located.
