## Q3242. Spectral versus growth bounds under eventual noncommutative positivity

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 19) · **Collection** 33

Must s(A)=ω₀(A)?

**Context.** Origin for question 3242: Problem posed by Ralph Chill, communicated by Sahiba Arora. Setup for question 3242: Let M be a von Neumann algebra with a normal semifinite faithful trace τ, and 1≤p<∞. On the noncommutative space L^p(M,τ), let (T(t))\_{t≥0} be a strongly continuous semigroup with generator A. Assume individual eventual positivity: every x≥0 has t₀(x) such that T(t)x≥0 whenever t≥t₀(x). Set s(A)=sup Re σ(A) and ω₀(A)=inf{ω∈R:sup_{t≥0}e^{−ωt}||T(t)||<∞}.

**Source.** Kodjo Raphaël Madou, Ivan Remizov and Reihaneh Vafadar, editors. *Open problems in one-parameter operator semigroups theory*. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.00416v2) Location: Problem 19, pp. 14–15

**Literature check.** Status for question 3242, checked 8 October 2026: Positive semigroups are covered by Coine–Laforge–Ricard (September 2026); the individually eventually positive case remains unlocated.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.opso2024.fsg.ulaval.ca/open-problems) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09339v2)


<a id="q3247"></a>

## Q3247. Norm-dense linear spaces avoiding hypercyclicity

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 2.2) · **Collection** 33

Must L(X) contain an operator-norm dense linear subspace V such that no nonzero T∈V is hypercyclic?

**Context.** Origin for question 3247: Original Problem 2.2; the paper’s dense-subspace theorems use the weaker strong operator topology. Setup for question 3247: Let X be an infinite-dimensional real or complex Banach space and L(X) its bounded operators with the operator norm. An operator is hypercyclic if one vector has a norm-dense forward orbit.

**Source.** N. G. Albuquerque; G. Araújo; L. Bernal-González; E. Demétrio Jr.; J. Seoane-Sepúlveda. *SOT-large subspaces of non-cyclic operators*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27211v1) Location: Problem 2.2, p. 5; dense-lineability definition p. 3

**Literature check.** Status for question 3247, checked 8 October 2026: No exact later resolution located; checked 8 October 2026.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00020-026-02852-y)


<a id="q3248"></a>

## Q3248. Density of mixing compact-shift perturbations of identity

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5.9) · **Collection** 33

Are the mixing operators in M dense in M?

**Context.** Origin for question 3248: Original Question 5.9; generic weak mixing proved in the same paper does not imply topological mixing. Setup for question 3248: Fix 1&lt;p<∞ and X=ℓ^p(Z) over R or C. For a scalar sequence w∈c₀(Z), meaning w_n→0 as |n|→∞, define (B_wx)\_n=w_{n+1}x_{n+1}. Let M={I+B_w:w∈c₀(Z)} have the operator-norm topology. An operator T is mixing if for all nonempty norm-open U,V⊂X there is N with T^n(U)∩V≠∅ for every n≥N.

**Source.** Jian Li; Qijing Liao. *Typical dynamical properties of operators on ℓᵖ*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.10957v2) Location: Question 5.9, p. 30; shift definition p. 7

**Literature check.** Status for question 3248, checked 8 October 2026: No exact later resolution located; checked 8 October 2026.


<a id="q3249"></a>

## Q3249. Geodesic spaces and weak almost squareness

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 33

Must F(M) be weakly almost square?

**Context.** Origin for question 3249: Kaasik–Veeorg 2023 question, retained in thesis §2.1, p. 16. Setup for question 3249: Let (M,d,0) be a complete pointed geodesic metric space: every two points are joined by a path whose length is their distance. Let Lip₀(M) be the real Lipschitz functions vanishing at 0, with Lipschitz norm, and let F(M)=closure span{δ\_x:x∈M}⊂Lip₀(M)\*, where δ\_x(f)=f(x). A Banach space X is weakly almost square if every x∈S_X admits (y_n)⊂B_X with y_n⇀0, ||y_n||→1, and ||x±y_n||→1.

**Source.** Jaan Kristjan Kaasik. *Diameter two properties and almost square properties in Lipschitz-free spaces and spaces of Lipschitz functions*. 2026. Advisor(s): Rainis Haller and Andre Ostrak. [primary source](https://dspace.ut.ee/server/api/core/bitstreams/c02176e5-c010-4413-8393-399be9f6cfff/content) Location: §2.1, p. 16, discussion before Proposition 2.1.5; definition §1.2.4, p. 11

**Literature check.** Status for question 3249, checked 8 October 2026: Only finitely supported x are covered by the thesis proposition. No later exact resolution located; checked 8 October 2026.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.09158v2) · [2](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/S0022247X23003426) · [3](https://math.ut.ee/en/news/pictures-jaan-kristjan-kaasik-defended-his-doctoral-theses-diameter-two-properties-and-almost)


<a id="q3250"></a>

## Q3250. Schur p without strong Schur p in a free space

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.3) · **Collection** 33

For each 0&lt;p≤1, does a metric space M exist such that F_p(M) has Schur p but fails strong Schur p?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3250: Lipschitz Structure of Banach Spaces. Jan Bíma. Charles University PhD, defended 20 March 2026. Advisor: Marek Cúth. Origin for question 3250: Original Question 6.3, also reproduced as Bíma thesis Question 3.6.3. Setup for question 3250: For 0&lt;p≤1 and pointed metric M, F_p(M) is the real p-Banach completion of span{δ\_x}, δ\_0=0, with ||Σa_xδ\_x||=sup_f||Σa_xf(x)|| over pointed 1-Lipschitz maps into real p-Banach spaces. Schur p means every bounded uniformly separated infinite set contains an ℓ\_p-basis-equivalent sequence. Strong Schur p means some K>0 makes every normalized δ-separated sequence admit a subsequence (x_j) with ||Σa_jx_j||≥(δ/K)(Σ|a_j|^p)^{1/p} for every finitely supported real scalar sequence a.

**Source.** Fernando Albiac; José L. Ansorena; Jan Bíma; Marek Cúth. *Lipschitz free p-spaces for 0&lt;p<1 in the light of the Schur p-property and the compact reduction*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s12220-025-02260-5) Location: Question 6.3, p. 29; Definition 2.5, p. 7

**Literature check.** Status for question 3250, checked 8 October 2026: September sequel settles neighboring questions and the compact-base strong property; no exact general separation resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.10747v1) · [2](https://dspace.cuni.cz/handle/20.500.11956/207701?locale-attribute=en)


<a id="q3332"></a>

## Q3332. Projectivity of the quotient-boundary module

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 34

Is P projective in the category of all A-Hilbert modules?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3332: Mainak Bhowmik. Indian Institute of Science, Bengaluru, 2025; advisor: Tirthankar Bhattacharyya. Origin for question 3332: Thesis p. 176, Part II, third future question; associated 2024 paper proves only normal-category projectivity. Setup for question 3332: Fix integers d≥2 and t|m, m,t≥1. Let θ\_j(z)=e_j(z₁^m,…,z_d^m) for j&lt;d and θ\_d(z)=(z₁⋯z_d)^(m/t), where e_j is elementary symmetric. Put Ω=θ(𝔻^d), ν=θ\_\*m_T, with m_T normalized Haar measure on 𝕋^d, A=H(Ω)∩C(Ω̄) with sup norm, and P=L²(θ(𝕋^d),ν), acted on by multiplication. An A-Hilbert module has jointly continuous unital module action; morphisms are bounded module maps. Projectivity means every short exact module sequence 0→H→K→P→0 splits by a bounded module map.

**Source.** Mainak Bhowmik. *Herglotz representation, approximation by inner functions, and Hankel operators*. Indian Institute of Science, Bengaluru, 2025. Advisor(s): Tirthankar Bhattacharyya. [primary source](https://drive.google.com/file/d/1Zz8k_5RFSiXBbFkfc_b7UZ7asMLlK6AY/view) Location: p. 176, Part II.3; Definitions 8.1,8.5, pp. 149–152

**Literature check.** Status for question 3332, checked 8 October 2026: Thesis target remains open; current 2409.04582v1 treats only the normal category.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.04582) · [2](https://sites.google.com/view/mainakbhowmik-iisc/home) · [3](https://etd.iisc.ac.in/handle/2005/7115)

## Q3333. Attainable complexity ranks

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.4) · **Collection** 34

Which ordinals occur as cr(A)?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 3333, 3334, 3335: Finite-Dimensional Approximations and C\*-algebraic K-theory. Arturo Jaime. University of Hawaiʻi at Mānoa, 2025; advisor: Rufus Willett. Origin for questions 3333, 3334, 3335: Paper Questions 6.4,6.5,6.7; doctoral-linked prior paper, not asserted thesis-origin. Setup for questions 3333, 3334, 3335: All algebras are unital C\*-algebras. A decomposes over 𝒞 if every finite F⊂A and ε>0 admit subalgebras C,D,E∈𝒞 containing 1_A and 0≤h≤1 in E with ||[h,x]||<ε, dist(hx,C),dist((1−h)x,D),dist(h(1−h)x,E)<ε for x∈F, and dist(e,C),dist(e,D)<ε for e∈B_E. Let 𝒟₀ be the locally finite-dimensional algebras; for α>0, 𝒟\_α consists of algebras decomposable over ⋃\_{β<α}𝒟\_β. Write cr(A)=min{α:A∈𝒟\_α}. Weak rank≤1 uses finite-dimensional C,D containing 1_A, a positive contraction h∈A, and only the commutator and two one-sided approximation conditions above. Real rank zero means finite-spectrum selfadjoints are dense in A_sa.

**Source.** Arturo Jaime; Rufus Willett. *Complexity rank for C\*-algebras*. 2023. [primary source](https://par.nsf.gov/servlets/purl/10428533) Location: Question 6.4, p. 91

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3333, 3334, 3335, checked 8 October 2026: Later strong-rank and AT claims do not settle these retained targets.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.uni-muenster.de/FB10/mjm/vol_16/mjm_vol_16_03.pdf) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12089v2) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.09809v1) · [4](https://math.hawaii.edu/~jaime/Jaime_CV.pdf)

## Q3344. Haagerup approximation for general Hecke algebras

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 9.1) · **Collection** 34

Must M have the Haagerup property?

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 3344, 3345: On rigidity theory, strong solidity, Coxeter groups, graph products and commutator estimates. Matthijs Jacobus Borst. Delft University of Technology, 2025; advisors: Martijn Caspers, Mark Veraar. Origin for questions 3344, 3345: Paper Problem 9.1, p. 34; thesis Problem 4.8.1, p. 90. Setup for questions 3344, 3345: Let (W,S) be a finite-rank Coxeter system and q_s>0, constant on conjugate generators. On ℓ²(W), define T_sδ\_w=δ\_sw if |sw|>|w| and T_sδ\_w=δ\_sw+(q_s−1)/√q_s·δ\_w otherwise. Put M={T_s:s∈S}″ and τ(x)=⟨xδ\_e,δ\_e⟩. Haagerup property means normal unital τ-preserving completely positive maps approximating identity pointwise in ||·||₂ and inducing compact L²(M,τ) operators. Weak-\* CBAP means normal finite-rank completely bounded maps Φ\_i with sup_i||Φ\_i||\_cb<∞ and Φ\_i(x)→x ultraweakly.

**Source.** Matthijs Borst; Martijn Caspers; Mateusz Wasilewski. *Bimodule coefficients, Riesz transforms on Coxeter groups and strong solidity*. 2024. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2109.00588v3) Location: Problem 9.1, p. 34; thesis4.8.1, p. 90

**Literature check.** Status for questions 3344, 3345, checked 8 October 2026: Corrected v3 and thesis retain both; later graph-product results cover special cases.

**Further links.** [1](https://repository.tudelft.nl/record/uuid:d63bdb80-fffa-4b97-85ba-7dc663e80721) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2110.15078) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00194)

## Q3346. Diagonal dimension under intermediate algebras

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 34

If D is separable and D⊂B⊂A, must dim_diag(D⊂B)≤dim_diag(D⊂A)?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 3346: Regularity properties of C\*-diagonal pairs and topological dynamics. Grigorios (Grigoris) Kopsacheilis. Universität Münster, 2025; advisor: Wilhelm Winter. Origin for question 3346: Remark 4.2, pp. 24–25; later paper after the2025 doctorate. Setup for question 3346: A C\*-diagonal D⊂A is a masa with faithful conditional expectation A→D, unique extensions of pure states, and N_A(D)={v:vDv\*∪v\*Dv⊂D} generating A. Define dim_diag(D⊂A)≤d when identity admits arbitrarily accurate point-norm factorizations A→ψ F→φ A with finite-dimensional F=⊕\_{i=0}^d F_i, masas D_F=⊕D_i, ψ completely positive contractive, ψ(D)⊂D_F, and each φ|F_i completely positive contractive, preserving orthogonality of positive elements, and carrying N_{F_i}(D_i) into N_A(D). The dimension is the least d, or ∞.

**Source.** Grigoris Kopsacheilis. *Diagonal dimension and intermediate sub-C\*-algebras*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24699v1) Location: Remark 4.2, p. 24; Definition 1.2, p. 5

**Literature check.** Status for question 3346, checked 8 October 2026: Current v1 retains general monotonicity; totally disconnected spectra are settled.

## Q3420. CSS\* non-inner amenability

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 9.2) · **Collection** 35

Must Γ be non-inner amenable?

**Context.** Doctoral context: Eli Mackenzie Bashwinger. UAlbany, 2024; advisors: Matthew Zaremsky, Jon Bannon. Origin: Question 9.2 in current revised paper; later doctoral-linked work. Setup: Let X be infinite compact ultrametric. Each pair of positive-radius closed balls B,C has a countable set Sim(B,C) of surjective similarities, containing identities and closed under inverse, composition, and restriction. Let Γ comprise homeomorphisms locally restricting to these similarities. Assume every infinite ball contains two disjoint infinite subballs, and every proper ball B embeds by Sim into a subball of every infinite proper ball D. Inner amenability means a conjugation-invariant finitely additive probability m on all subsets of Γ with m({g})=0 for each g. Supporting sources: https://sites.google.com/view/eli-bashwinger/ https://scholarsarchive.library.albany.edu/legacy-etd/3288/

**Source.** Eli Bashwinger; Patrick DeBonis. *On Similarity Structure Groups and their W\* and C\*-Algebras*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.18821v2) Location: Question 9.2, p. 47; Definitions 2.5–2.8, pp. 9–10.

**Literature check.** Status (2026-10-09): Current v2 explicitly asks this question; its scope is narrower than the v1 abstract’s claim.

## Q3450. Finite support of extreme points in combinatorial spaces

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 4.0.1) · **Collection** 35

Must every extreme point of B have finite support?

**Context.** Degree award: https://bip.uni.wroc.pl/download/attachment/51430/uchwala-nr-5-2026-w-sprawie-nadania-stopnia-doktora-mgr-sebastian-jachimek.pdf, Resolution 5/2026, §1. Setup: Let F be a hereditary family of finite subsets of N containing every singleton. Let X_F be the completion of real finitely supported sequences under ‖x‖\_F=sup_{A∈F}Σ\_{i∈A}|xᵢ|. A point x of its closed unit ball B is extreme when x=(y+z)/2, y,z∈B, implies y=z=x.

**Source.** Sebastian Jachimek. *Combinatorial Banach Spaces*. University of Wrocław, 2025. Advisor(s): Piotr Borodulin-Nadzieja. [primary source](https://bip.uni.wroc.pl/download/attachment/50013/rozprawa-doktorska-s_jachimek.pdf) Location: Chapter 4, p. 65, paragraph following Problem 4.0.1.

**Literature check.** Status checked 9 October 2026: No resolution located through 2026-10-09. May 2026 graph-duality paper distinguishes the larger Y_F from X_F; its infinite-support examples concern Y_F.

**Notes.** Origin: Explicit unresolved doctoral question; general extreme-point classification is not separately counted.

**Further links.** [1](https://bip.uni.wroc.pl/download/attachment/51430/uchwala-nr-5-2026-w-sprawie-nadania-stopnia-doktora-mgr-sebastian-jachimek.pdf)


<a id="q3451"></a>

## Q3451. An ℓ₁-sum of c₀ inside the Sierpiński space

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5.9) · **Collection** 35

Does X_f contain a closed linear subspace isomorphic to ℓ₁(c₀)?

**Context.** Doctoral context: Piotr Borodulin-Nadzieja, University of Wrocław PhD 2007; advisors Grzegorz Plebanek. Thesis: On Measures on Polish Spaces and on Boolean Algebras. Setup: Fix a bijection f:N→Q. On real finitely supported sequences define ‖x‖\_f=sup{Σᵢ₌₁ᵏ|x(nᵢ)|:k≥1,n₁<⋯&lt;nₖ,f(n₁)<⋯&lt;f(nₖ)}; let X_f be its completion. Let ℓ₁(c₀) consist of sequences (yₙ) of real null sequences with Σₙ‖yₙ‖∞<∞, with norm equal to that sum.

**Source.** Piotr Borodulin-Nadzieja, Barnabás Farkas and Anna Pelczar-Barwacz. *Geometric Duality, Perfect Graphs, and the Sierpiński Space*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.14072v1) Location: Question 5.9, p. 16; definition§5, p. 12; Theorem 5.3 makes the isomorphism type independent of f.

**Literature check.** Status checked 9 October 2026: arXiv v1 remains latest; targeted later-paper/errata searches through 2026-10-09 found no resolution.

**Notes.** Origin: Borodulin-Nadzieja–Farkas–Pelczar-Barwacz 2026; reached through Jachimek’s advisor genealogy, not a 2007 doctoral question.


<a id="q3452"></a>

## Q3452. Exponent independence of groupoid K-theory

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 2.4.8) · **Collection** 35

For every such G, every p,q∈[1,∞), and i∈{0,1}, are Kᵢ(Fλᵖ(G)) and Kᵢ(Fλᑫ(G)) isomorphic?

**Context.** Setup: Let G be a locally compact Hausdorff groupoid whose source s and range r are local homeomorphisms. For a unit u put Gᵤ=s⁻¹(u). For 1≤p<∞, complete the complex compactly supported continuous convolution algebra Cc(G) in ‖f‖p=supᵤ‖λᵤᵖ(f)‖B(ℓᵖ(Gᵤ)), where (λᵤᵖ(f)ξ)(x)=Σy∈Gᵤ f(xy⁻¹)ξ(y). Denote this Banach algebra by Fλᵖ(G), and its topological K-groups by K₀ and K₁.

**Source.** Mathias Sperre Palmstrøm. *On operator algebras associated with étale groupoids: Exotic C\*-completions, K-theory invariance, and rigidity*. Norwegian University of Science and Technology (NTNU), 2025. Advisor(s): Eduard Ortega (primary), Are Austad (co-supervisor), Franz Luef (co-supervisor). [primary source](https://nva.sikt.no/registration/0198ec0ad1e9-096d369a-774a-478e-a74a-74110f2e5a3b) Location: Question 2.4.8, printed p. 56 (PDF p. 66); definitions §2.4.1.

**Literature check.** Status checked 9 October 2026: No general resolution located through 2026-10-09. The published 2026 growth theorem covers strong subexponential growth. The September 2026 Morita paper compares equivalent groupoids at fixed p and treats p-independence for proper actions.

**Literature check.** Status sources: https://ems.press/content/serial-article-files/52761, Theorem A; https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.39031v1, §8.1.

**Notes.** Origin: Explicit doctoral question extending earlier special cases.

**Further links.** [1](https://ems.press/content/serial-article-files/52761) · [2](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.39031v1)


<a id="q3453"></a>

## Q3453. Existence of intermediate groupoid C\*-norms

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 2.3.1) · **Collection** 35

If ‖·‖max≠‖·‖r, must a C\*-norm N on Cc(G) exist with ‖f‖r≤N(f)≤‖f‖max for every f, such that N differs from each endpoint norm?

**Context.** Doctoral context: Palmstrøm, above. Setup: Let G be a second-countable locally compact Hausdorff groupoid with source and range local homeomorphisms. On complex Cc(G), use (f\*g)(x)=Σy∈Gₛ₍ₓ₎f(xy⁻¹)g(y) and f\*(x)=overline{f(x⁻¹)}. Put ‖f‖I=max{supᵤΣx∈Gᵤ|f(x)|,supᵤΣx∈Gᵘ|f(x)|}, where Gᵤ=s⁻¹(u),Gᵘ=r⁻¹(u). The full norm ‖f‖max is the supremum of ‖π(f)‖ over I-contractive Hilbert-space \*-representations; the reduced norm is ‖f‖r=supᵤ‖λᵤ(f)‖, where λᵤ is convolution on ℓ²(Gᵤ).

**Source.** Mathias Palmstrøm. *Exotic C\*-completions of étale groupoids*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2311.12428v5) Location: Accepted arXiv v5, Introduction p. 1; definitions §2.3; thesis Question 2.3.1, p. 29.

**Literature check.** Status checked 9 October 2026: Unresolved in the inspected accepted version; no later general resolution or erratum located through 2026-10-09. Its existence theorem requires additional metric and growth assumptions.

**Notes.** Origin: Author’s doctoral-era groupoid question, extending older group questions.


<a id="q3454"></a>

## Q3454. Norm continuity of Moore–Penrose inversion

**Status:** Open, partial results · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2.22) · **Collection** 35

If Moore–Penrose invertible elements aₙ converge in norm to a Moore–Penrose invertible a and supₙ‖aₙ†‖<∞, must ‖aₙ†−a†‖→0?

**Context.** Doctoral context: Palmstrøm, above. Setup: Let A be a complex unital Banach algebra with ‖1‖=1. An element h is hermitian when ‖exp(ith)‖=1 for every real t. An element a is Moore–Penrose invertible if there is b∈A with aba=a, bab=b, and ab and ba hermitian. Such b is unique; write a†=b.

**Source.** Eusebio Gardella, Mathias Palmstrøm and Hannes Thiel. *Rigidity of pseudofunction algebras of ample groupoids*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.09563v1) Location: Conjecture 2.22, p. 8; definitions 2.1–2.7, pp. 4–5.

**Literature check.** Status checked 9 October 2026: The current preprint proves the claim for Lp-operator algebras and further specified classes, while retaining the general conjecture. No general resolution or later revision located through 2026-10-09.

**Notes.** Origin: Rakočević’s 1988 conjecture, restated in the joint paper and thesis Conjecture E.2.14; not a newly originated doctoral claim.

**Result (2026-10-09).** When corresponding initial support idempotents commute and corresponding final support idempotents commute, M||a-b||<1 forces equal supports and ||b^dagger-a^dagger|| <= ||b^dagger||||a^dagger||||a-b||, where M=max(||a^dagger||,||b^dagger||). The unrestricted noncommuting-support conjecture remains open. The result removes the ultrahermitian assumption in the specified commuting setting; strict separation of algebra classes is not claimed.

[Proof](../../solutions/round-2026-10-09/algebra/Q3454-proof.md) · [Internal review](../../solutions/round-2026-10-09/reviews/root-review.md) · [Exact computational controls](../../solutions/round-2026-10-09/algebra/verification.json). This is a research proposal with a separate internal AI review, not an externally peer-reviewed result.

## Q3534. Weighted rotations without supercyclic vectors

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.5.2) · **Collection** 36

For 1&lt;p<∞, φ∈L∞[0,1] and α∈[0,1], must Tφ,αf(t)=φ(t)f({t+α}) on Lp[0,1] fail to be supercyclic? Here {·} is fractional part.

**Context.** Degree: doctorate Advisors: Sophie Grivaux. Source type: PhD dissertation; accepted-paper crosschecks Origin: Thesis questions restate the author’s Bishop and Collatz papers. Setup: Use complex spaces. Supercyclicity means having a dense projective orbit {λTⁿx:λ∈C,n≥0}. Let A²(D) be the square-integrable holomorphic functions on the unit disc. On A²(D)/span{1,z,z²}, let C(zⁿ)=z^{c(n)} modulo this span, where c(n)=n/2 for even n and (3n+1)/2 for odd n.

**Source.** Vincent Béhani. *Étude de la Conjecture de Syracuse et des opérateurs de Bishop du point de vue de la dynamique linéaire*. Université de Lille, 2023. Advisor(s): Sophie Grivaux. [primary source](https://pepite-depot.univ-lille.fr/LIBRE/EDMADIS/2023/2023ULILB025.pdf) Location: Thesis Question 4.5.2, p.127.

**Literature check.** Status: No resolution located in the bounded October 2026 check.

## Q3536. Frequent recurrence under inversion

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 4) · **Collection** 36

Must the inverse of every frequently recurrent continuous linear automorphism be frequently recurrent?

**Context.** Degree: doctorate Advisors: Alfred Peris Manguillot, Sophie Grivaux. Source type: PhD dissertation; later final reaffirmation Origin: Thesis Conclusions, Open Problem 4; earlier joint recurrence paper. Setup: An F-space is a completely metrizable topological vector space. Use separable infinite-dimensional real or complex F-spaces. A vector x is frequently recurrent for T if, for every neighborhood U of x, liminf_{N→∞} |{0≤n&lt;N:Tⁿx∈U}|/N>0. An operator is frequently recurrent when such vectors are dense.

**Source.** Antoni López-Martínez. *Combinatorial Number Theory, Recurrence of Operators and Linear Dynamics*. Universitat Politècnica de València, 2023. Advisor(s): Alfred Peris Manguillot, Sophie Grivaux. [primary source](https://riunet.upv.es/server/api/core/bitstreams/f2691b55-3a05-4aff-84c6-9c6a418bd95a/content) Location: Thesis p.167, Problem 4(a); JMPA 169 (2023), Question 8.8.

**Literature check.** Status: Direct-square question reaffirmed in 2025; no inverse resolution found. Inspected 2026 backward-shift results are narrower.

## Q3542. Regularity from antisymmetric pieces

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5.14) · **Collection** 36

If A|M is regular for every maximal A-antisymmetric set M, must A be regular on X?

**Context.** Degree: PhD Advisors: Joel F. Feinstein. Source type: Final paper; thesis record and advisor verified Origin: Feinstein–Morley joint paper containing work from Morley’s doctorate. Setup: A uniform algebra is a uniformly closed complex subalgebra A⊂C(X), for compact Hausdorff X, containing constants and separating points. A set M is A-antisymmetric if every a∈A real-valued on M is constant there. A|M is regular when every closed E⊂M and x∈M\E admit a∈A with a|E=0 and a(x)=1.

**Source.** Joel F. Feinstein and Sam Morley. *Quasianalyticity in certain Banach function algebras*. University of Nottingham, 2017. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.4064/sm8614-12-2016) Location: Quasianalyticity in certain Banach function algebras, Studia Math. 238 (2017), Question 5.14(a), p.151.

## Q3550. Pre-regular vector spaces without quasi-regularity

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 36

Does a pre-regular real mixed lattice vector space exist which is not quasi-regular?

**Context.** Degree: doctorate Advisors: Lassi Paunonen. Source type: PhD dissertation Origin: Dissertation Conclusions Question 1; already posed in Eriksson–Jokela’s 2021 paper. Setup: A mixed lattice group is an Abelian group with translation-invariant partial orders ≤,≼ for which x∧ₘy=max≤{z:z≼x,z≤y} and x∨ₘy=min≤{z:x≼z,y≤z} exist. Put C={x:0≼x}. Pre-regular means C⊂{x:0≤x}; quasi-regular means C is closed under both mixed operations; regular means quasi-regular and G=C−C. For real vector spaces both orders must also respect multiplication by nonnegative scalars.

**Source.** Jani Jokela. *Mixed Lattice Structures*. Tampere University, 2024. Advisor(s): Lassi Paunonen. [primary source](https://trepo.tuni.fi/bitstream/10024/155949/2/978-952-03-3407-9.pdf) Location: Conclusions Question 1, p.38; definitions pp.20–24.

**Literature check.** Status: The 2026 final mixed-order paper was inspected; its discrete and irregular examples do not settle this question.

**Further links.** [1](https://trepo.tuni.fi/handle/10024/155949)

## Q3648. Diametral slices without diametral weak neighborhoods

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 2) · **Collection** 37

Does a real Banach space with DLD2P but without DD2P exist?

**Context.** Doctoral route: Diametral diameter two properties, Daugavet-, and Δ-points in Banach spaces; University of Tartu; 2020 Advisors: Trond Arnold Abrahamsen, Rainis Haller, Johann Langemets Setup: For a real Banach space X, let B_X and S_X denote its closed unit ball and unit sphere. Slices are S(x\*,α)={x∈B_X:x\*(x)>1−α}, where ||x\*||=1 and α>0. Say DLD2P holds if sup_{y∈S}||x−y||=2 for every slice S and x∈S∩S_X; DD2P replaces slices by all nonempty relatively weakly open subsets of B_X. Origin: Thesis Problem 2, restating Becerra Guerrero–López-Pérez–Rueda Zoca.

**Source.** Katriin Pirk. *Diametral diameter two properties, Daugavet-, and Δ-points in Banach spaces*. University of Tartu, 2020. Advisor(s): Trond Arnold Abrahamsen, Rainis Haller, Johann Langemets. [primary source](https://dspace.ut.ee/server/api/core/bitstreams/10294299-6d8d-424c-b0e1-9b006462fd84/content) Location: Perspectives, Problem 2, p.46.

**Literature check.** Status: Reaffirmed explicitly in July 2026 arXiv:2607.04370, p.2.


<a id="q3651"></a>

## Q3651. Strong regularity with the super alternative Daugavet property

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.2) · **Collection** 37

Does an infinite-dimensional strongly regular Banach space with the super ADP exist?

**Context.** Paper: The super Alternative Daugavet property for Banach spaces; 2026 final Setup: For a real or complex Banach space X, super ADP means sup_{y∈W}max_{|θ|=1}||x+θy||=2 for every x∈S_X and nonempty relatively weakly open W⊂B_X meeting S_X. Strong regularity means every nonempty closed bounded convex C⊂X has finite convex combinations of slices of arbitrarily small diameter. A slice is {x∈C:Re f(x)>sup_C Re f−α}, with 0≠f∈X\* and α>0. Origin: Final article Question 6.2; reached through Pirk’s advisor Langemets.

**Source.** Johann Langemets, Marcus Lõo, Miguel Martín, Yoël Perreau and Abraham Rueda Zoca. *The super Alternative Daugavet property for Banach spaces*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1017/S0013091525101168) Location: Question 6.2, p.497; Definition 1.2, p.472.

**Literature check.** Status: July 2026 unconditional-basis restrictions do not settle strong regularity.

## Q3743. If A∈C₁ and Ã vanishes at infinity, does some compact K satisfy K̃=Ã?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1) · **Collection** 38

If A∈C₁ and Ã vanishes at infinity, does some compact K satisfy K̃=Ã?

**Context.** Robert Fulsche: Hannover PhD, Toeplitz operators and generated algebras on non-Hilbertian spaces (2020); advisor Wolfram Bauer. Origin: Fulsche–Hagger, §5, refining Hagger’s earlier Questions 32–33. In L²(C,π⁻¹e^{−|z|²}dA), put F_k=ker(∂̄^k)⊖ker(∂̄^{k−1}), k≥2, with orthogonal projection P_k. Let W_zf(w)=e^{w z̄−|z|²/2}f(w−z), C₁={A∈B(F_k):||W_zAW_z\*−A||→0 as z→0}, and T_a=P_kM_a|\_{F_k}, where M_af=af. For normalized reproducing kernels k_z, write Ã(z)=⟨Ak_z,k_z⟩. BUC means bounded uniformly continuous functions.

**Source.** Fulsche and Hagger. *Quantum Harmonic Analysis for Polyanalytic Fock Spaces*. 2024. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00041-024-10124-9) Location: Question 1, p. 38

**Literature check.** Status: Final JFAA 30:63 (2024) inspected.

## Q3747. For every reflexive Banach space X, every nonempty closed bounded convex C⊂X, and every…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 38

For every reflexive Banach space X, every nonempty closed bounded convex C⊂X, and every nonexpansive T:C→C, must T have a fixed point?

**Context.** Enrique Llorens-Fuster–Elena Moreno Gálvez, Opuscula Mathematica (2026), DOI10.7494/OpMath.202511141. Origin: Classical reflexive fixed-point problem, reaffirmed in the introduction. Dissertation search did not authenticate Stawski’s doctoral metadata. A map T:C→C is nonexpansive when ||Tx−Ty||≤||x−y|| for all x,y∈C.

**Source.** Enrique Llorens-Fuster and Elena Moreno Gálvez. *Fixed Point Theory for Jaggi L-type Mappings*. 2026. [primary source](https://www.opuscula.agh.edu.pl/onlinefirst-art/opuscula_math_202511141.pdf) Location: Introduction, p. 1
